Equivariant Eilenberg-Watts theorems for locally compact quantum groups
Joeri De Ro

Abstract
. Given two von Neumann algebras A A and B B , the W ∗ W^{*} -algebraic Eilenberg-Watts theorem, due to M. Rieffel, asserts that there is a canonical equivalence Corr ⁡ ( A , B ) ≃ Fun ⁡ ( Rep ⁡ ( B ) , Rep ⁡ ( A ) ) \mathrm{Corr}(A,B)\simeq\Fun(\Rep(B),\Rep(A)) of categories, where Corr ⁡ ( A , B ) \mathrm{Corr}(A,B) denotes the category of all A A - B B -correspondences, Rep ⁡ ( A ) \Rep(A) is the category of all unital normal ∗ * -representations of A A on Hilbert spaces and Fun ⁡ ( Rep ⁡ ( B ) , Rep ⁡ ( A ) ) \Fun(\Rep(B),\Rep(A)) denotes the category of all normal ∗ * -functors Rep ⁡ ( B ) → Rep ⁡ ( A ) \Rep(B)\to\Rep(A) . In this paper, we upgrade the von Neumann algebras A A and B B with actions A ↶ G A\curvearrowleft\mathbb{G} and B ↶ G B\curvearrowleft\mathbb{G} of a locally compact quantum group G \mathbb{G} , and we provide several equivariant versions of the W ∗ W^{*} -algebraic Eilenberg-Watts theorem using the language of module categories. We also prove that for a locally compact quantum group G \mathbb{G} with Drinfeld double D ⁡ ( G ) D(\mathbb{G}) , the category of unitary D ⁡ ( G ) D(\mathbb{G}) -representations is isomorphic to the Drinfeld center of Rep ⁡ ( G ) \Rep(\mathbb{G}) , generalizing a result by Neshveyev-Yamashita from the compact to the locally compact setting.

1.
Introduction

Given von Neumann algebras
A
and
B
, let us write
\mathrm{Corr}(A,B)
for the
W^{*}
-category of
A
-
B
-correspondences (sometimes also called
A
-
B
-bimodules), that is Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{H}
endowed with commuting unital, normal
*
-representations of
A
and
B^{\operatorname{op}}

[
Con80
]
. We also write
\Rep(A)=\mathrm{Corr}(A,\mathbb{C})
for the
W^{*}
-category of unital, normal
*
-representations of
A
on Hilbert spaces. It follows from the seminal work
[
Rie74
]
(see also
[
Sau83
,
Bro03
]
), that there is a canonical equivalence

\mathrm{Corr}(A,B)\simeq\Fun(\Rep(B),\Rep(A)):\mathcal{G}\mapsto(F_{\mathcal{G}}:\mathcal{H}\mapsto\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H})

(1.1)

of categories (Theorem
2.5
), where
\Fun(\Rep(B),\Rep(A))
denotes the category of normal
*
-functors
\Rep(B)\to\Rep(A)
. This is a
W^{*}
-algebraic version of the celebrated Eilenberg-Watts theorem
[
Ei60
,
Wa60
]
.

In
[
DCDR24
]
, the notion of an
A
-
B
-correspondence was generalized to the equivariant setting, where the von Neumann algebras
A
and
B
are upgraded with actions of a locally compact quantum group
\mathbb{G}
. This leads to the
W^{*}
-category
\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
of
\mathbb{G}
-
A
-
B
-correspondences, whose objects consist of
A
-
B
-correspondences
\mathcal{H}
that are upgraded with a unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation compatible with the relevant structures. We also write
\Rep{G}(A):=\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,\mathbb{C})
. It is then natural to ask if the equivalence (
1.1
) has appropriate analogons in the equivariant setting, where the left hand side is replaced by
\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
.

This was first investigated in
[
DR25a
]
, where it was argued that it is necessary to take into account the extra module-categorical structure that the equivariant setting offers. More precisely, it was observed in
[
DR25a
]
that every
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra
A\curvearrowleft\mathbb{G}
induces a natural action
\Rep{G}(A)\curvearrowleft\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, turning
\Rep{G}(A)
in a right
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
-
W^{*}
-module category. This then leads to the canonical functor

\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\to\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A)):\mathcal{G}\mapsto(F_{\mathcal{G}}:\mathcal{H}\mapsto\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H}),

(1.2)

where
\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))
is the category of normal
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
-module
*
-functors
\Rep{G}(B)\to\Rep{G}(A)
. It was shown in
[
DR25a
]
*Theorem 4.5 that (
1.2
) is an equivalence of categories when
\mathbb{G}
is a
compact
quantum group, but it was left open if (
1.2
) also defines an equivalence of categories for a general locally compact quantum group. In this paper, we settle this question in the affirmative. Reasoning along the same lines, we will also show that
\Rep(A\rtimes\mathbb{G})\curvearrowleft\Rep(\mathbb{G})
in a natural way, and that there is a natural equivalence

\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\simeq\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep(B\rtimes\mathbb{G}),\Rep(A\rtimes\mathbb{G})).

These are all
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
-module versions of the equivariant Eilenberg-Watts theorem (see Theorem
3.8
).

Given a
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra
\alpha:A\curvearrowleft\mathbb{G}
,
a universal version of the coaction
\alpha:A\curvearrowleft\mathbb{G}
was constructed in the recent paper
[
DCK24
]
. Denoting the dual locally compact quantum group of
\mathbb{G}
by
\check{\mathbb{G}}
, we use this construction to prove that there is a natural action
\Rep(A)\curvearrowleft\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
, turning
\Rep(A)
into a right
\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
-
W^{*}
-module category. We then prove that there is a natural equivalence

\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\simeq\Fun_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(\Rep(B),\Rep(A)),

providing also a
\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
-module version of the equivariant Eilenberg-Watts theorem (see Theorem
4.8
). The analogon of this fact in the setting of finite-dimensional Hopf algebras was already established in
[
AM07
]
*Proposition 1.23. Moreover, it is worth noting that taking
A=B=\mathbb{C}
endowed with the trivial
\mathbb{G}
-actions leads to the equivalence

\Rep(\mathbb{G})\simeq\End_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(\Hilb),

where the
W^{*}
-category
\Hilb
of Hilbert spaces is acted upon by
\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
through the forgetful functor
\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})\to\Hilb
(see also
[
EGNO15
]
*Example 7.12.26 for the analogon in the setting of finite-dimensional Hopf algebras).

Given a tensor category
\mathscr{C}
, we denote its Drinfeld center by
\mathcal{Z}(\mathscr{C})
(see
[
EGNO15
]
*Subsection 7.13 or
[
Kas95
]
*XIII.4).
If
H
is a finite-dimensional Hopf algebra, consider the tensor category
\prescript{}{H}{\operatorname{Mod}}
of left
H
-modules. It is well-known (see e.g.
[
Kas95
]
*Theorem XIII.5.1) that

\mathcal{Z}(\prescript{}{H}{\operatorname{Mod}})\cong\prescript{}{D(H)}{\operatorname{Mod}},

where
D(H)
is the Drinfeld double of
H
. An analogon of this fact was proven in the setting of compact quantum groups in
[
NY18
]
*Section 3, where the authors prove that

\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))\cong\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))

for
\mathbb{G}
a compact quantum group with Drinfeld double
D(\mathbb{G})
(note that the Drinfeld center is defined using
unitary
half-braidings in this setting). In the last section of this paper, we generalize this result to the setting of locally compact quantum groups (see Theorem
5.3
and Proposition
5.4
).

2.
Preliminaries

All vector spaces in this paper are defined over the field of complex numbers. We assume that inner products of Hilbert spaces are anti-linear in the first variable. Given a subset
S
of a normed linear space
V
, we write
[S]
for the norm-closure of the linear span of
S
. More generally, if
(V,\tau)
is a topological vector space and
S\subseteq V
, we write
[S]^{\tau}
for the
\tau
-closure of
S
inside
V
. Given
C^{*}
-algebras
C,D
, their minimal tensor product is denoted by
C\otimes D
. The multiplier
C^{*}
-algebra of
C
is denoted by
M(C)
. Given von Neumann algebras
A
and
B
, their von Neumann algebra tensor product is denoted by
A\bar{\otimes}B
. Given Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}
, we write
\Sigma=\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}:\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K}\to\mathcal{K}\otimes\mathcal{H}
for the switch map.

2.1.
W*-categories

For the standard definition of a
C^{*}
-category, we refer the reader to
[
GLR85
]
*Definition 1.1.

A linear functor
F:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
between
C^{*}
-categories is called
*
-functor
if
F(f^{*})=F(f)^{*}
for all
X,Y\in\mathscr{C}
and all
f\in\mathscr{C}(X,Y)
.

A
W^{*}
-category

\mathscr{C}
is a
C^{*}
-category such that for every two objects
X,Y\in\mathscr{C}
, the Banach space
\mathscr{C}(X,Y)
has a predual. In particular, the morphism spaces
\mathscr{C}(X)=\mathscr{C}(X,X)
are
W^{*}
-algebras. A
*
-functor
F:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
between
W^{*}
-categories is called
normal
if for every object
X\in\mathscr{C}
, the unital
*
-homomorphism

\mathscr{C}(X)\to\mathscr{D}(F(X)):f\mapsto F(f)

is normal. We write
\Fun(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{D})
for the class of all normal
*
-functors
\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
. Two
W^{*}
-categories
\mathscr{C},\mathscr{D}
are called
equivalent
if there exist normal
*
-functors
F:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
and
G:\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{C}
together with natural unitary isomorphisms
F\circ G\cong\id_{\mathscr{D}}
and
G\circ F\cong\id_{\mathscr{C}}.

Given a
W^{*}
-category
\mathscr{C}
, a
direct sum
of a set of objects
\{C_{i}\}_{i\in I}\subseteq\mathscr{C}
is an object
C=\bigoplus_{i\in I}C_{i}\in\mathscr{C}
together with isometries
\lambda_{i}:C_{i}\hookrightarrow C
such that
\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{i}^{*}=1_{C}
(the convergence of the sum is to be understood in the
\sigma
-weak topology of
\mathscr{C}(C)
). Assume that
\mathscr{C},\mathscr{D}
are
W^{*}
-categories and that
F:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}
is a normal
*
-functor. If
(C,\{\lambda_{i}:C_{i}\to C\}_{i\in I})
is a direct sum of
\{C_{i}\}_{i\in I}
, then
(F(C),\{F(\lambda_{i}):F(C_{i})\to F(C)\}_{i\in I})
is a direct sum of
\{F(C_{i})\}_{i\in I}
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a
W^{*}
-category admitting (arbitrary) direct sums. We call
C\in\mathscr{C}
a
generator
, if for every object
D\in\mathscr{C}
, there exists an index set
I
and an isometry
D\hookrightarrow\bigoplus_{i\in I}C
.

For much more information about
W^{*}
-categories, the reader is referred to
[
GLR85
]
.

We now discuss an important example of a
W^{*}
-category. Given a von Neumann algebra
A
, we write
\Rep(A)
for the
W^{*}
-category of unital, normal
*
-representations of
A
on Hilbert spaces. Its objects consist of pairs
(\mathcal{H},\pi_{\mathcal{H}})
where
\mathcal{H}
is a Hilbert space and
\pi_{\mathcal{H}}:A\to B(\mathcal{H})
is a unital, normal
*
-homomorphism. Often, we will simply write
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(A)
, leaving the representation
\pi_{\mathcal{H}}:A\curvearrowright\mathcal{H}
implicit. Given
\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\in\Rep(A)
, the associated morphism space is

{}_{A}\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}):=\{x\in B(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime})\mid\forall a\in A:x\pi_{\mathcal{H}}(a)=\pi_{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}(a)x\}.

Let us recall some results about this
W^{*}
-category that will be relevant to us. An object
(\mathcal{H},\pi)\in\Rep(A)
is a generator if and only if
\pi
is faithful
[
Rie74
]
*Proposition 1.3. If
B
is another von Neumann algebra,
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B)
is a generator and
F,G\in\Fun(\Rep(B),\Rep(A))
, then the map

\operatorname{Nat}(F,G)\to\{x\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}(F(\mathcal{H}),G(\mathcal{H}))\mid\forall y\in{}_{B}\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{H}):xF(y)=G(y)x\}:(\eta_{\mathcal{K}})_{\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(B)}\mapsto\eta_{\mathcal{H}}

(2.1)

is a well-defined bijection
[
Rie74
]
*Proposition 5.4, where
\operatorname{Nat}(F,G)
denotes the set of natural transformations
F\implies G
.

If
\mathcal{H}=(\mathcal{H},\pi_{\mathcal{H}})\in\Rep(B)
and if
\mathcal{K}
is an arbitrary Hilbert space, then
(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K},b\mapsto\pi_{\mathcal{H}}(b)\otimes 1)\in\Rep(B)
. Given a normal
*
-functor
F:\Rep(B)\to\Rep(A)
, there then is a canonical
A
-linear isomorphism

C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}^{F}:F(\mathcal{H})\otimes\mathcal{K}\to F(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K}).

(2.2)

More concretely, fix an orthonormal basis
\{e_{i}\}_{i\in I}
for
\mathcal{K}
, and consider for every
i\in I
the induced
B
-linear isometry

\iota_{i}:\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K}:\xi\mapsto\xi\otimes e_{i}.

Then

C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}^{F}(\xi\otimes e_{i})=F(\iota_{i})\xi,\quad\xi\in F(\mathcal{H}),\quad i\in I

defines an
A
-linear unitary, and it does not depend on the choice of orthonormal basis
\{e_{i}\}_{i\in I}
for
\mathcal{K}
. Given
F,G\in\Fun(\Rep(B),\Rep(A))
and
\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}(F,G)
, it is then easily verified that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H})\otimes\mathcal{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H})\otimes\mathcal{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}^{G}}

(2.3)

commutes.

2.2.
Locally compact quantum groups

Given a von Neumann algebra
A
, we consider the standard Hilbert space
L^{2}(A)
with modular conjugation
J_{A}
. It carries a unital, normal, faithful
*
-representation
\pi_{A}:A\to B(L^{2}(A))
and a unital, normal, faithful anti-
*
-representation
\rho_{A}:A\to B(L^{2}(A)):x\mapsto J_{A}\pi_{A}(x)^{*}J_{A}
. We have
\rho_{A}(A)=\pi_{A}(A)^{\prime}
.

In this subsection, we recall some theory of locally compact quantum groups
[
KV00
,
KV03
,
VV03
]
.

A
von Neumann bialgebra
is a pair
(M,\Delta)
where
M
is a von Neumann algebra and
\Delta:M\to M\bar{\otimes}M
is a unital, normal, isometric
*
-homomorphism such that
(\Delta\otimes\id)\circ\Delta=(\id\otimes\Delta)\circ\Delta
.

A
locally compact quantum group

\mathbb{G}
is a von Neumann bialgebra
(L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}),\Delta)
for which there exist normal, semifinite, faithful weights
\varphi,\psi:L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})_{+}\to[0,\infty]
such that
(\id\otimes\varphi)\Delta(x)=\varphi(x)1
for all
x\in\mathscr{M}_{\varphi}^{+}
and
(\psi\otimes\id)\Delta(x)=\psi(x)1
for all
x\in\mathscr{M}_{\psi}^{+}
.

Without any loss of generality, we may (and will) assume that
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is faithfully represented in standard form on the Hilbert space
L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
. The standard predual of
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
will be denoted by
L^{1}(\mathbb{G})
. With respect to the weights
\varphi,\psi
, we then have GNS-maps

\Lambda_{\varphi}:\mathscr{N}_{\varphi}\to L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\quad\Lambda_{\psi}:\mathscr{N}_{\psi}\to L^{2}(\mathbb{G}).

Fundamental to the theory of locally compact quantum groups are the unitaries

V,W\in B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))

called respectively
right
and
left
regular unitary representation. They are uniquely characterized by the identities

\displaystyle(\id\otimes\omega)(V)\Lambda_{\psi}(x)

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{\psi}((\id\otimes\omega)\Delta(x)),\qquad\omega\in L^{1}(\mathbb{G}),\quad x\in\mathscr{N}_{\psi},

\displaystyle(\omega\otimes\id)(W^{*})\Lambda_{\phi}(x)

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{\phi}((\omega\otimes\id)\Delta(x)),\qquad\omega\in L^{1}(\mathbb{G}),\quad x\in\mathscr{N}_{\phi}.

They are
multiplicative unitaries

[
BS93
]
meaning that

V_{12}V_{13}V_{23}=V_{23}V_{12},\qquad W_{12}W_{13}W_{23}=W_{23}W_{12},

and they implement the coproduct of
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
in the sense that

W^{*}(1\otimes x)W=\Delta(x)=V(x\otimes 1)V^{*},\qquad x\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}).

Moreover, we have

C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}):=[(\omega\otimes\id)(V)\mid\omega\in B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*}]=[(\id\otimes\omega)(W)\mid\omega\in B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*}],

which is a
\sigma
-weakly dense C
∗
-subalgebra of
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
. Then
\Delta(C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}))\subseteq M(C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}))
.

We also define the von Neumann algebras

\displaystyle L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}):=[(\omega\otimes\id)(W)\mid\omega\in B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*}]^{\sigma\textrm{-weak}},\quad L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}):=[(\id\otimes\omega)(V)\mid\omega\in B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*}]^{\sigma\textrm{-weak}}

together with the coproducts

\displaystyle\hat{\Delta}:L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\to L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}):\hat{x}\mapsto\Sigma W(\hat{x}\otimes 1)W^{*}\Sigma,\quad\check{\Delta}:L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\to L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}):\check{x}\mapsto V^{*}(1\otimes\check{x})V.

The pairs
(L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}),\hat{\Delta})
and
(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}),\check{\Delta})
then define locally compact quantum groups. We have
L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})=L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{\prime}
. If
G
is a classical locally compact group, we have

L^{\infty}(\hat{G})=\mathscr{L}(G)
and
L^{\infty}(\check{G})=\mathscr{R}(G)
, the left resp. right group von Neumann algebra associated with
G
. Therefore,
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
and
\check{\mathbb{G}}
should be thought of as a left and a right version of the dual locally compact quantum group.

The von Neumann algebras
L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})
and
L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})
, acting naturally on
L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
, are in standard form. We have

\check{W}=V\in L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}),\quad W\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}),\quad\check{V}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime}\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}),\quad\hat{W}=W_{21}^{*}\in L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}).

In fact, we even have
V\in M(C_{0}^{r}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}))
and
W\in M(C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}))
.

Consider the modular conjugation
J:=J_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}
associated to the standard representation
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\subseteq B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
and the modular conjugation
\check{J}:=J_{L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})}
associated to the standard representation
L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\subseteq B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
. We will write
\rho{G}(x):=\rho_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}(x)=Jx^{*}J
for
x\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
to denote the standard anti-
*
-representation. We have

\check{J}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\check{J}=L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}),

so we obtain the anti-
*
-morphism

R:L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\to L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}):x\mapsto\check{J}x^{*}\check{J}.

We call
R
the
unitary antipode
of
\mathbb{G}
. There is a canonical unimodular complex number
c\in\mathbb{C}
such that

c\check{J}J=\overline{c}J\check{J}.

We write
u{G}:=c\check{J}J
for the associated self-adjoint unitary. The following identities will be useful:

\displaystyle(\check{J}\otimes J)W(\check{J}\otimes J)=W^{*},\quad(J\otimes\check{J})V(J\otimes\check{J})=V^{*},\quad(u{G}\otimes 1)V(u{G}\otimes 1)=W_{21}.

Pontryagin biduality holds:
\hat{\hat{\mathbb{G}}}=\mathbb{G}
and
\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}}\cong\mathbb{G}
via the isomorphism
x\mapsto u{G}xu{G}
.

Consider the (unbounded) antipode
S:\mathcal{D}(S)\to L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
of
\mathbb{G}
and define

L^{1}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{G}):=\{\omega\in L^{1}(\mathbb{G})\mid\exists\omega^{*}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{G}):\forall x\in\mathcal{D}(S):\omega^{*}(x)=\overline{\omega(S(x)^{*})}\}.

The subspace
L_{\sharp}^{1}(\mathbb{G})
is norm-dense in
L^{1}(\mathbb{G})
. The assignment
\omega\mapsto\omega^{*}
turns
L_{\sharp}^{1}(\mathbb{G})
into a Banach
*
-algebra for the norm
\|\omega\|_{\sharp}:=\max\{\|\omega\|,\|\omega^{*}\|\}
, and we define
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
to be the
C^{*}
-completion of
L_{\sharp}^{1}(\mathbb{G})
with respect to the norm

\|\omega\|_{u}:=\sup_{\phi}\|\phi(\omega)\|,\quad\omega\in L^{1}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{G}),

where
\phi
ranges over all non-degenerate
*
-representations of
L_{\sharp}^{1}(\mathbb{G})
on Hilbert spaces.

2.3.
Representation theory of locally compact quantum groups.

A unitary (right)
\mathbb{G}
-representation consists of a pair
(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}})
where
\mathcal{H}
is a Hilbert space and
U_{\mathcal{H}}\in B(\mathcal{H})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is a unitary satisfying
(\id\otimes\Delta)(U_{\mathcal{H}})=U_{\mathcal{H},12}U_{\mathcal{H},13}
. To simplify notation, we will often simply write
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
or
U_{\mathcal{H}}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
. In fact, we automatically have
U_{\mathcal{H}}\in M(\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}))
. Note that
(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),W_{21})\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
. When we write
L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, we will always assume that it is endowed with the right regular representation
V
, unless explicitly mentioned otherwise. If
\mathcal{H}
is any Hilbert space, then
\mathbb{I}=\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{H}}:=1\otimes 1\in B(\mathcal{H})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is called the
trivial

\mathbb{G}
-representation on
\mathcal{H}
. If
(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}})
and
(\mathcal{K},U_{\mathcal{K}})
are unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representations, then so are
(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}})\otop(\mathcal{K},U_{\mathcal{K}}):=(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K},U_{\mathcal{H},13}U_{\mathcal{K},23})
and
(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\otop$}}}}\mkern-2.0mu(\mathcal{K},U_{\mathcal{K}}):=(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K},U_{\mathcal{K},23}U_{\mathcal{H},13})
. Given
\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, we write

\mathscr{L}{G}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K})=\mathscr{L}{G}(U_{\mathcal{H}},U_{\mathcal{K}}):=\{x\in B(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}):(x\otimes 1)U_{\mathcal{H}}=U_{\mathcal{K}}(x\otimes 1)\}

to denote the space of
\mathbb{G}
-intertwiners
. In this way, we obtain the
W^{*}
-category
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
. Given
\omega\in L^{1}(\mathbb{G})
, we will use the notation
U_{\mathcal{H}}(\omega):=(\id\otimes\omega)(U_{\mathcal{H}})
.

The following well-known fact will be absolutely crucial to obtain our main results. Its proof consists of elementary calculations.

Proposition 2.1

(Fell’s absorption principle)
.

Let
(\mathcal{H},U)
be a unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation. Then

\displaystyle U\in\mathscr{L}{G}(U_{13}V_{23},V_{23}),\quad(u{G}\otimes 1)U_{21}^{*}(u{G}\otimes 1)\in\mathscr{L}{G}(V_{13}U_{23},V_{13}).

The
C^{*}
-algebra
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
governs the representation theory of
\mathbb{G}

[
Kus01
]
. More precisely, given a unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation
\mathcal{H}=(\mathcal{H},U)
, there is a unique non-degenerate
*
-representation

\phi_{\mathcal{H}}=\phi_{U}:C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\to B(\mathcal{H})
such that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\omega)=(\id\otimes\omega)(U)
for all
\omega\in L^{1}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{G})
. The assignment
\mathcal{H}\mapsto\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
sets up a bijective correspondence between the unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representations and the non-degenerate
*
-representations of
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})
on Hilbert spaces (see subsection
4.1
for more details).

The following is well-known:

Proposition 2.2
.

Given a unital, normal
*
-representation
\pi:L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\to B(\mathcal{H})
, there exists a unique unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation
U_{\pi}\in B(\mathcal{H})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
such that
\pi(V(\omega))=U_{\pi}(\omega)
for all
\omega\in L^{1}(\mathbb{G})
. The assignment
(\mathcal{H},\pi)\mapsto(\mathcal{H},U_{\pi})
defines a fully faithful normal
*
-functor
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))\to\Rep(\mathbb{G})
that it is the identity on morphisms.

Proof.

Consider the canonical map
\pi_{\check{\mathbb{G}}}=\phi_{V}:C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\to C_{0}^{r}(\check{\mathbb{G}})
uniquely determined by
\pi_{\check{\mathbb{G}}}(\omega)=(\id\otimes\omega)(V)
for
\omega\in L_{\sharp}^{1}(\mathbb{G})
.
The composition
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{\check{\mathbb{G}}}}}{{\to}}C_{0}^{r}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\subseteq L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}B(\mathcal{H})

is a non-degenerate
*
-representation, and thus induces the required
\mathbb{G}
-representation
U_{\pi}
. The remainder of the proposition is trivial.
∎

Proposition
2.2
is illustrated by the following table, where
U\in B(\mathcal{H})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
denotes an arbitrary
\mathbb{G}
-representation:

\mathcal{K}

\pi:L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\to B(\mathcal{K})

U_{\pi}\in B(\mathcal{K})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})

L^{2}(\mathbb{G})

\pi(\check{x})=\check{x}

V

L^{2}(\mathbb{G})

\pi(\check{x})=u{G}\check{x}u{G}

W_{21}

\mathcal{H}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})

\pi(\check{x})=U^{*}(1\otimes\check{x})U

U_{13}V_{23}

L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathcal{H}

\pi(\check{x})=(u{G}\otimes 1)U_{21}(u{G}\check{x}u{G}\otimes 1)U_{21}^{*}(u{G}\otimes 1)

V_{13}U_{23}

From now on, we will always identify
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))\subseteq\Rep(\mathbb{G})
. This inclusion is strict if and only if
\mathbb{G}
is non-compact, see
[
DR25a
]
*Proposition 4.1, Remark 4.2.

2.4.
Actions and crossed products

A right
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra
consists of a pair
(A,\alpha)
where
A
is a von Neumann algebra and
\alpha:A\to A\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is a unital, normal, isometric
*
-homomorphism satisfying the coaction property
(\id\otimes\Delta)\alpha=(\alpha\otimes\id)\alpha
. We will work exclusively with right
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebras in this paper, so we leave out the prefix ‘right’ from the terminology, and we will often employ the more intuitive notation
\alpha:A\curvearrowleft\mathbb{G}
.

If
(A,\alpha)
is a
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra, we define the
crossed product von Neumann algebra

A\rtimes_{\alpha}\mathbb{G}:=A^{\rtimes}:=[\alpha(A)(1\otimes L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))]^{\sigma\text{-weak}}\subseteq A\bar{\otimes}B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})).

We obtain the induced
dual
action
\alpha^{\rtimes}:A^{\rtimes}\curvearrowleft\check{\mathbb{G}}
by

\alpha^{\rtimes}:A^{\rtimes}\to A^{\rtimes}\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}):z\mapsto\check{V}_{23}z_{12}\check{V}_{23}^{*}.

It satisfies
\alpha^{\rtimes}(\alpha(a))=\alpha(a)\otimes 1
and
\alpha^{\rtimes}(1\otimes\check{y})=1\otimes\check{\Delta}(\check{y})
for all
a\in A
and
\check{y}\in L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})
.

There exists a canonical unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation
U_{\alpha}\in B(L^{2}(A))\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
such that

(\pi_{A}\otimes\id)(\alpha(a))=U_{\alpha}(\pi_{A}(a)\otimes 1)U_{\alpha}^{*}
for all
a\in A

[
Va01
]
. We call
U_{\alpha}
the
unitary implementation
of
\alpha
. In fact, we can identify
L^{2}(A^{\rtimes})=L^{2}(A)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
in such a way that the standard
*
-representation
\pi_{A^{\rtimes}}
is exactly
\pi_{A}\otimes\id
and such that the anti-
*
-representation
\rho_{A^{\rtimes}}
is given by

\rho_{A^{\rtimes}}(\alpha(a))=\rho_{A}(a)\otimes 1,\quad\rho_{A^{\rtimes}}(1\otimes\check{y})=U_{\alpha}(1\otimes\rho_{\check{\mathbb{G}}}(\check{y}))U_{\alpha}^{*},\quad a\in A,\quad\check{y}\in L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}).

Given a
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra
(A,\alpha)
, we associate to it the
W^{*}
-category
\Rep{G}(A)
. Its objects consist of triples
\mathcal{H}=(\mathcal{H},\pi,U)
where
\mathcal{H}
is a Hilbert space,
\pi:A\to B(\mathcal{H})
is a unital, normal
*
-representation and
U\in B(\mathcal{H})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is a unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation satisfying
(\pi\otimes\id)(\alpha(a))=U(\pi(a)\otimes 1)U^{*}
for all
a\in A
. For example,
L^{2}(A)=(L^{2}(A),\pi_{A},U_{\alpha})\in\Rep{G}(A)
. Given
\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\in\Rep{G}(A)
, the associated morphism space of intertwiners is denoted by

{}_{A}\mathscr{L}{G}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime})
and consists of all bounded linear operators
x:\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
satisfying
x\pi(a)=\pi^{\prime}(a)x
for all
a\in A
and
(x\otimes 1)U=U^{\prime}(x\otimes 1)
.

Given
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep{G}(A)
, we see that

S{G}(\mathcal{H}):=(\mathcal{H}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),a\mapsto U_{\mathcal{H}}(\pi_{\mathcal{H}}(a)\otimes 1)U_{\mathcal{H}}^{*}=(\pi_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\id)(\alpha(a)),V_{23})\in\Rep{G}(A).

(2.4)

We have

{}_{A}\mathscr{L}{G}(S{G}(\mathcal{H}))=(\pi_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\id)(A^{\rtimes})^{\prime}.

(2.5)

The following is proven in
[
DR25a
]
*Lemma 3.8:

Proposition 2.3
.

Let
(A,\alpha)
be a
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra.
Given
(\mathcal{H},\kappa)\in\Rep(A^{\rtimes})
, there exists a unique
(\mathcal{H},\pi_{\kappa},U_{\kappa})\in\Rep{G}(A)
such that

\kappa(\alpha(a))=\pi_{\kappa}(a),\quad\kappa(1\otimes V(\omega))=U_{\kappa}(\omega),\quad a\in A,\quad\omega\in L^{1}(\mathbb{G}).

The assignment
(\mathcal{H},\kappa)\mapsto(\mathcal{H},\pi_{\kappa},U_{\kappa})
defines a fully faithful normal
*
-functor
\Rep(A^{\rtimes})\to\Rep{G}(A)
that is the identity on morphisms.

In what follows, we will always identify
\Rep(A^{\rtimes})\subseteq\Rep{G}(A)
. If
(\mathcal{H},\kappa)\in\Rep(A^{\rtimes})
, we will sometimes write
\kappa=\pi_{\kappa}\rtimes U_{\kappa}
. For example, the standard representation
\pi_{A}\otimes\id:A^{\rtimes}\to B(L^{2}(A)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
of
A^{\rtimes}
is
(\pi_{A}\otimes\id)\alpha\rtimes V_{23}
.

2.5.
Equivariant correspondences

In this subsection, we recall some theory from the article
[
DCDR24
]
.

Let
(A,\alpha)
and
(B,\beta)
be
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebras. A
\mathbb{G}
-
A
-
B
-correspondence
consists of the data
(\mathcal{H},\pi,\rho,U)
where
\mathcal{H}
is a Hilbert space,
\pi:A\to B(\mathcal{H})
is a unital, normal
*
-representation,
\rho:B\to B(\mathcal{H})
is a unital, normal, anti-
*
-representation and
U\in B(\mathcal{H})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is a unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation such that

\pi(a)\rho(b)=\rho(b)\pi(a),\quad(\pi\otimes\id)(\alpha(a))=U(\pi(a)\otimes 1)U^{*},\quad(\rho\otimes R)(\beta(b))=U^{*}(\rho(b)\otimes 1)U

for all
a\in A
and
b\in B
. Often, we will simply write
\mathcal{H}\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
, and leave the representation theoretic data
\pi,\rho,U
implicit.
A
\mathbb{G}
-
A
-
B
-correspondence
(\mathcal{H},\pi,\rho,U)
is called
\mathbb{G}
-
A
-
B
-
Morita correspondence
if
\pi
and
\rho
are faithful and if
\pi(A)^{\prime}=\rho(B)
. If such a Morita correspondence exists, then
(A,\alpha)
and
(B,\beta)
are called
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-Morita equivalent and we write
(A,\alpha)\sim{G}(B,\beta)
. We refer the reader to
[
DR25a
,
DR25b
]
for more information about this notion.

If
\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
, we write
{}_{A}\mathscr{L}_{B}{G}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime})
for the space of bounded linear operators
x:\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
such that
x\pi(a)=\pi^{\prime}(a)x,x\rho(b)=\rho^{\prime}(b)x
and
(x\otimes 1)U=U^{\prime}(x\otimes 1)
for all
a\in A
and all
b\in B
. In this way,
\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
becomes a
W^{*}
-category. Note also that taking
\mathbb{G}
to be the trivial group, we simply recover the category
\mathrm{Corr}(A,B)
of
A
-
B
-correspondence as introduced by Connes
[
Con80
]
.

Example 2.4
.

Let
(A,\alpha)
and
(B,\beta)
be two given
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebras.

(1)

The trivial
\mathbb{G}
-
A
-
A
-correspondence is
L^{2}(A):=(L^{2}(A),\pi_{A},\rho_{A},U_{\alpha})
.

(2)

The semi-coarse
\mathbb{G}
-
A
-
A
-correspondence is
S_{A}{G}:=(L^{2}(A)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),(\pi_{A}\otimes\id)\alpha,(\rho_{A}\otimes\rho{G})\alpha,V_{23}).

There are some non-trivial ways to construct new equivariant correspondences from given ones. We end this subsection by discussing two such constructions.

Crossed products of equivariant correspondences

[
DCDR24
]
*Proposition 5.21. Given
\mathcal{H}=(\mathcal{H},\pi,\rho,U)\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
, we define
\mathcal{H}^{\rtimes}:=(\mathcal{H}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\pi^{\rtimes},\rho^{\rtimes},\check{V}_{23})\in\mathrm{Corr}^{\check{\mathbb{G}}}(A^{\rtimes},B^{\rtimes})
, where

\pi^{\rtimes}(s):=(\pi\otimes\id)(s),\quad\rho^{\rtimes}(t)=U(\rho\otimes\check{J}(-)^{*}\check{J})(t)U^{*},\quad s\in A^{\rtimes},\quad t\in B^{\rtimes}.

Composition of equivariant correspondences

[
DCDR24
]
*Proposition 5.6. There is a natural operation

\boxtimes_{B}:\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\times\mathrm{Corr}{G}(B,C)\to\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,C).

More concretely, if
\mathcal{H}=(\mathcal{H},\pi_{\mathcal{H}},\rho_{\mathcal{H}},U_{\mathcal{H}})\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
and
\mathcal{K}=(\mathcal{K},\pi_{\mathcal{K}},\rho_{\mathcal{K}},U_{\mathcal{K}})\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(B,C)
, then
\mathscr{L}_{B}(L^{2}(B),\mathcal{H})\curvearrowleft B
via
xb:=x\pi_{B}(b)
and
B\curvearrowright\mathcal{K}
via
b\xi:=\pi_{\mathcal{K}}(b)\xi
where
b\in B,x\in\mathscr{L}_{B}(L^{2}(B),\mathcal{H})
and
\xi\in\mathcal{K}
. It thus makes sense to form the (algebraic) balanced tensor product
\mathscr{L}_{B}(L^{2}(B),\mathcal{H})\odot_{B}\mathcal{K}
, and we endow it with the semi-inner product uniquely determined by

\langle x\otimes_{B}\xi,x^{\prime}\otimes_{B}\xi^{\prime}\rangle:=\langle\xi,\pi_{\mathcal{K}}(\langle x,x^{\prime}\rangle_{B})\xi^{\prime}\rangle,\quad x,x^{\prime}\in\mathscr{L}_{B}(L^{2}(B),\mathcal{H}),\quad\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K},

where
\langle x,x^{\prime}\rangle_{B}
is the unique element of
B
satisfying
\pi_{B}(\langle x,x^{\prime}\rangle_{B})=x^{*}x^{\prime}
. By separation-completion, we obtain the Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{K}
. It carries a natural
A
-
C
-correspondence structure, given by

\pi_{\boxtimes}(a)\rho_{\boxtimes}(c)(x\otimes_{B}\xi):=\pi_{\mathcal{H}}(a)x\otimes_{B}\rho_{\mathcal{K}}(c)\xi,\quad a\in A,\quad c\in C,\quad x\in\mathscr{L}_{B}(L^{2}(B),\mathcal{H}),\quad\xi\in\mathcal{K}.

Further, viewing
\mathcal{H}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\in\mathrm{Corr}(A\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}),B\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))
and
\mathcal{K}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\in\mathrm{Corr}(B\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}),C\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))
in the obvious way, we have

\mathscr{L}_{B\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}(L^{2}(B\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})),\mathcal{H}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))=\mathscr{L}_{B\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}(L^{2}(B)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathcal{H}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))=\mathscr{L}_{B}(L^{2}(B),\mathcal{H})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}),

together with the natural identification

(\mathcal{H}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{K})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\to(\mathcal{H}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\boxtimes_{B\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}(\mathcal{K}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})):(x\otimes_{B}\xi)\otimes\eta\mapsto(x\otimes 1)\otimes_{B\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}(\xi\otimes\eta)

(2.6)

of
A\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
-
C\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
-correspondences. The unitary

U_{\boxtimes}:(\mathcal{H}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{K})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\to(\mathcal{H}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\boxtimes_{B\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}(\mathcal{K}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\cong(\mathcal{H}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{K})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})

uniquely determined by

U_{\boxtimes}((x\otimes_{B}\xi)\otimes\eta)=U_{\mathcal{H}}(x\otimes 1)U_{\beta}^{*}\otimes_{B\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}U_{\mathcal{K}}(\xi\otimes\eta)

endows the Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{K}
with a
\mathbb{G}
-representation such that
\mathcal{H}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{K}:=(\mathcal{H}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{K},\pi_{\boxtimes},\rho_{\boxtimes},U_{\boxtimes})\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,C)
. We refer to this operation as the
Connes fusion tensor product
of equivariant correspondences. The Connes fusion tensor product is associative in a natural way.

2.6.
The W*-algebraic Eilenberg-Watts theorem

We believe it is beneficial for the reader to recall the (non-equivariant)
W^{*}
-algebraic Eilenberg-Watts theorem, due to M. Rieffel
[
Rie74
]
. Note that instead of the Hilbert space picture of correspondences, Rieffel rather works in the equivalent framework of (self-dual) Hilbert
W^{*}
-
A
-
B
-bimodules
[
BDH88
]
*Théorème 2.2.

Let
A,B
be von Neumann algebras. If
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Corr}(A,B)
, define a functor
P(\mathcal{G}):=F_{\mathcal{G}}\in\Fun(\Rep(B),\Rep(A))
as follows:

•

If
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B)=\mathrm{Corr}(B,\mathbb{C})
, put
F_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{H})=\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H}\in\mathrm{Corr}(A,\mathbb{C})=\Rep(A)
.

•

If
\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\in\Rep(B)
and
x\in{}_{B}\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime})
, define

F_{\mathcal{G}}(x):=1\boxtimes_{B}x:\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\quad F_{\mathcal{G}}(x)(y\otimes_{B}\xi):=y\otimes_{B}x\xi,\quad y\in\mathscr{L}_{B}(L^{2}(B),\mathcal{G}),\quad\xi\in\mathcal{H}.

If
\mathcal{G},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Corr}(A,B)
,
x\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}_{B}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B)
, we define the
A
-linear morphism

P(x)_{\mathcal{H}}:\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H},\quad P(x)_{\mathcal{H}}(y\otimes_{B}\xi)=xy\otimes_{B}\xi,\quad y\in\mathscr{L}_{B}(L^{2}(B),\mathcal{G}),\quad\xi\in\mathcal{H}.

Then
P(x)=\{P(x)_{\mathcal{H}}\}_{\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B)}\in\operatorname{Nat}(F_{\mathcal{G}},F_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})
, and in this way we obtain the functor

P:\mathrm{Corr}(A,B)\to\Fun(\Rep(B),\Rep(A)).

On the other hand, if
F\in\Fun(\Rep(B),\Rep(A))
, then put
(\mathcal{G}_{F},\pi_{F}):=F((L^{2}(B),\pi_{B}))\in\Rep(A)
. If
b\in B
, then
\rho_{B}(b)\in\pi_{B}(B)^{\prime}={}_{B}\mathscr{L}(L^{2}(B))
, so it makes sense to define

\rho_{F}(b):=F(\rho_{B}(b))\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G}_{F}).

Thus, by construction,
\rho_{F}(b)\pi_{F}(a)=\pi_{F}(a)\rho_{F}(b)
for all
a\in A
and all
b\in B
, so that
(\mathcal{G}_{F},\pi_{F},\rho_{F})\in\mathrm{Corr}(A,B)
. If
F,G\in\Fun(\Rep(B),\Rep(A))
and
\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}(F,G)
, then

\eta_{L^{2}(B)}\rho_{F}(b)=\eta_{L^{2}(B)}F(\rho_{B}(b))=G(\rho_{B}(b))\eta_{L^{2}(B)}=\rho_{G}(b)\eta_{L^{2}(B)},

so it is clear that
\eta_{L^{2}(B)}\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}_{B}(\mathcal{G}_{F},\mathcal{G}_{G})
. In this way, we obtain the functor

Q:\Fun(\Rep(B),\Rep(A))\to\mathrm{Corr}(A,B).

Theorem 2.5

(W*-algebraic Eilenberg-Watts theorem)
.

The canonical functors

\displaystyle P:\mathrm{Corr}(A,B)\to\Fun(\Rep(B),\Rep(A)):\mathcal{G}\mapsto F_{\mathcal{G}}:(\mathcal{H}\mapsto\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H}),

\displaystyle Q:\Fun(\Rep(B),\Rep(A))\to\mathrm{Corr}(A,B):F\mapsto F(L^{2}(B))

are quasi-inverse to each other.

Proof.

Given
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Corr}(A,B)
, it is clear that
\rho_{F_{\mathcal{G}}}(b)=\rho_{\boxtimes}(b)
for
b\in B
, so we have the unitary isomorphism

QP(\mathcal{G})=\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}L^{2}(B)\cong\mathcal{G}:y\otimes_{B}\xi\mapsto y\xi

of
A
-
B
-correspondences. Thus, it is clear that there is a natural unitary isomorphism
Q\circ P\cong\id_{\mathrm{Corr}(A,B)}
. On the other hand, the bijectivity of the map (
2.1
) (applied to the generator
\mathcal{H}=L^{2}(B)\in\Rep(B)
) can be restated by saying that
Q
is fully faithful, so
P
and
Q
must be quasi-inverse to each other.
∎

2.7.
W*-tensor and module categories

Definition 2.6
.

A
W^{*}
-tensor category consists of a
W^{*}
-category
\mathscr{C}
, a functor
\otimes:\mathscr{C}\times\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}
, which is a normal
*
-functor in each variable, and unitary isomorphisms

a_{X,Y,Z}:X\otimes(Y\otimes Z)\cong(X\otimes Y)\otimes Z,\quad X,Y,Z\in\mathscr{C},

called
associators
, which are natural in
X,Y,Z
and satisfy the pentagon axiom.

Note that we do not ask for the existence of a monoidal unit.

The following two examples are the main examples of
W^{*}
-tensor categories that are of interest for this paper:

Example 2.7
.

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a locally compact quantum group and consider the
W^{*}
-category
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
of unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representations. It becomes a
W^{*}
-tensor category for

(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}})\otop(\mathcal{K},U_{\mathcal{K}}):=(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K},U_{\mathcal{H},13}U_{\mathcal{K},23})

(on morphisms, the tensor product
\otop
agrees with the tensor product on the underlying category of Hilbert spaces).
The associators in
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
are the usual associators in the underlying category of Hilbert spaces, and we will always treat them as identities.

Example 2.8
.

We have already seen that
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))\subseteq\Rep(\mathbb{G})
. In fact, we even have that
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
is an
ideal
inside
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, in the sense that

\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))\otop\Rep(\mathbb{G})\subseteq\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})),\quad\Rep(\mathbb{G})\otop\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))\subseteq\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})).

Indeed, this follows from the table below Proposition
2.2
, together with the fact that the identity representation
L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\subseteq B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
(corresponding to the
\mathbb{G}
-representation
V
) generates
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
. In particular,
(\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})),\otop)
becomes a
W^{*}
-tensor category in its own right.

Definition 2.9
.

A braided
W^{*}
-tensor category
\mathscr{C}
is a
W^{*}
-tensor category
(\mathscr{C},\otimes)
together with unitary isomorphisms

\gamma_{X,Y}:X\otimes Y\to Y\otimes X,\quad X,Y\in\mathscr{C},

natural in
X,Y
, such that the braid relations (see
[
EGNO15
]
*Definition 8.1.1, up to a convention change for the associators) are satisfied.

We will encounter examples of braided
W^{*}
-tensor categories in Section
5
.

Definition 2.10
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a
W^{*}
-tensor category. By a (right)
W^{*}
-module category
\mathscr{M}\curvearrowleft\mathscr{C}
, we mean a
W^{*}
-category
\mathscr{M}
endowed with a functor

\lhd:\mathscr{M}\times\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{M}

and unitary isomorphisms

\phi_{M,X,Y}:M\lhd(X\otimes Y)\cong(M\lhd X)\lhd Y,\quad M\in\mathscr{M},\quad X,Y\in\mathscr{C},

natural in
M,X,Y
, such that:

(1)

For all
M\in\mathscr{M}
,
M\lhd-:\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{M}
defines a normal
*
-functor.

(2)

For all
X\in\mathscr{C}
,
-\lhd X:\mathscr{M}\to\mathscr{M}
defines a normal
*
-functor.

(3)

The following diagram commutes for all
M\in\mathscr{M}
and all
X,Y,Z\in\mathscr{C}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\lhd(X\otimes(Y\otimes Z))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(M\lhd X)\lhd(Y\otimes Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\lhd((X\otimes Y)\otimes Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(M\lhd(X\otimes Y))\lhd Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((M\lhd X)\lhd Y)\lhd Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\lhd{a_{X,Y,Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{M,X,Y\otimes Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{M\lhd X,Y,Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{M,X\otimes Y,Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{M,X,Y}\lhd 1}

Remark 2.11
.

All module categories in this paper will be categories of Hilbert spaces endowed with extra structure. The isomorphisms
\phi_{M,X,Y}
occurring in Definition
2.10
will always be the canonical associators in the underlying category of Hilbert spaces, and will therefore always be treated as identities (i.e. we will not care about bracketing).

Definition 2.12
.

Given a
W^{*}
-tensor category
\mathscr{C}
and two
W^{*}
-module categories
\mathscr{M}\curvearrowleft\mathscr{C}
and
\mathscr{N}\curvearrowleft\mathscr{C}
, a normal
\mathscr{C}
-module
*
-functor consists of a normal
*
-functor
F:\mathscr{M}\to\mathscr{N}
together with unitary isomorphisms

S_{M,X}:F(M\lhd X)\to F(M)\lhd X,\quad M\in\mathscr{M},\quad X\in\mathscr{C},

natural in
M,X
, such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(M\lhd(X\otimes Y))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F((M\lhd X)\lhd Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(M)\lhd(X\otimes Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F(M)\lhd X)\lhd Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(M\lhd X)\lhd Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\phi_{M,X,Y})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{M,X\otimes Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{M\lhd X,Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{F(M),X,Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{M,X}\lhd 1}

(2.7)

commutes for all
M\in\mathscr{M}
and all
X,Y\in\mathscr{C}
. We will write
\Fun_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})
for the class of normal
\mathscr{C}
-module
*
-functors.

Often, we will simply write
F\in\Fun_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})
, leaving the unitaries
\{S_{M,X}\}
implicit.

Definition 2.13
.

Given two
W^{*}
-module categories
\mathscr{M}\curvearrowleft\mathscr{C}
and
\mathscr{N}\curvearrowleft\mathscr{C}
and
F,G\in\Fun_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})
, we denote the collection of natural transformations
\eta:F\implies G
such that the diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(M\lhd X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(M)\lhd X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(M\lhd X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(M)\lhd X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{M\lhd X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{M,X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{M}\lhd 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{M,X}}

(2.8)

commute for all
M\in\mathscr{M}
and all
X\in\mathscr{C}
by
\operatorname{Nat}_{\mathscr{C}}(F,G)
.

Note that we should designate the unitaries
S_{X,M}
in (
2.8
) with a label referring to which functor they are associated. We will often refrain from doing this, for ease of notation.

Fix
F,G,H\in\Fun_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})
. Given
\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\mathscr{C}}(F,G)
and
\theta\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\mathscr{C}}(G,H)
, it is easily verified that
\theta\circ\eta:=(\theta_{M}\circ\eta_{M})_{M\in\mathscr{M}}\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\mathscr{C}}(F,H)
. Thus,
\Fun_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})
becomes a category in an obvious way.

Two
W^{*}
-module categories
\mathscr{M}\curvearrowleft\mathscr{C}
and
\mathscr{N}\curvearrowleft\mathscr{C}
are called
equivalent
if there exist
F\in\Fun_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})
and
G\in\Fun_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathscr{N},\mathscr{M})
together with unitary isomorphisms in
\operatorname{Nat}_{\mathscr{C}}(GF,\id_{\mathscr{M}})
and
\operatorname{Nat}_{\mathscr{C}}(FG,\id_{\mathscr{N}})
.
1
1

1

Here, the compositions
GF
and
FG
can be viewed as
\mathscr{C}
-module functors in a natural way.

Plenty of examples of
W^{*}
-module categories and their associated module
*
-functors will be discussed in the following sections.

3.
Equivariant Eilenberg-Watts theorem -
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
-module version

Throughout this section, let
\mathbb{G}
be a locally compact quantum group and let
(A,\alpha)
and
(B,\beta)
be
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebras.

3.1.
Module category associated to dynamical system

We start by recalling the construction of the
W^{*}
-module category
\Rep{G}(A)\curvearrowleft\Rep(\mathbb{G})
from
[
DR25a
]
.

Given
\mathcal{H}=(\mathcal{H},\pi_{\mathcal{H}},U_{\mathcal{H}})\in\Rep{G}(A)
and
\mathcal{K}=(\mathcal{K},U_{\mathcal{K}})\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, it is elementary to verify that
\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}:=(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K},\pi_{\mathcal{H}\rhd\mathcal{K}}:a\mapsto\pi_{\mathcal{H}}(a)\otimes 1,U_{\mathcal{H}}\otop U_{\mathcal{K}})\in\Rep{G}(A)
. If
x\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}{G}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime})
and
y\in\mathscr{L}{G}(\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime})
, then
x\lhd y:=x\otimes y\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}{G}(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime})
. In this way, we obtain the functor

\lhd:\Rep{G}(A)\times\Rep(\mathbb{G})\to\Rep{G}(A),

and it is also obvious that

\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime})=(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\quad\mathcal{H}\in\Rep{G}(A),\quad\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G}),

so that we obtain the right
W^{*}
-module category
\Rep{G}(A)\curvearrowleft\Rep(\mathbb{G})
.

The following can be seen as a generalization of the ideal property of
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))\subseteq\Rep(\mathbb{G})
(cfr. Example
2.8
):

Lemma 3.1
.

\Rep(A^{\rtimes})\lhd\Rep(\mathbb{G})\subseteq\Rep(A^{\rtimes})
and
\Rep{G}(A)\lhd\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))\subseteq\Rep(A^{\rtimes}).

Proof.

If
(\mathcal{H},U)\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, Fell’s absorption principle (Proposition
2.1
) shows that

\tilde{U}:=(u{G}\otimes 1)U_{21}^{*}(u{G}\otimes 1)\in\mathscr{L}{G}(V_{13}U_{23},V_{13}).

We then define the
*
-representation

A^{\rtimes}\to B(L^{2}(A)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathcal{H}):z\mapsto\tilde{U}_{23}^{*}((\pi_{A}\otimes\id)(z)\otimes 1)\tilde{U}_{23},

showing that
(L^{2}(A)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\pi_{A}\otimes\id)\lhd(\mathcal{H},U)\in\Rep(A^{\rtimes})
.
Since
(L^{2}(A)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\pi_{A}\otimes\id)
is a generator for
\Rep(A^{\rtimes})
, it follows that

\Rep(A^{\rtimes})\lhd\Rep(\mathbb{G})\subseteq\Rep(A^{\rtimes}).

On the other hand, if
(\mathcal{K},\pi_{\mathcal{K}},U_{\mathcal{K}})\in\Rep{G}(A)
, defining the
*
-representation

A^{\rtimes}\to B(\mathcal{K}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})):z\mapsto U_{\mathcal{K}}^{*}(\pi_{\mathcal{K}}\otimes\id)(z)U_{\mathcal{K}}

shows that
(\mathcal{K},\pi_{\mathcal{K}},U_{\mathcal{K}})\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\in\Rep(A^{\rtimes})
. Since
(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)
is a generator for
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
, it follows that
\Rep{G}(A)\lhd\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))\subseteq\Rep(A^{\rtimes})
.
∎

By considering the restriction
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))\subseteq\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, we can also view
\Rep{G}(A)
as a right
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
-module
W^{*}
-category and
\Rep(A^{\rtimes})
as a right
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
-module
W^{*}
-category. In conclusion, to every
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra
A
, we have associated four module
W^{*}
-categories, namely

\Rep{G}(A)\curvearrowleft\Rep(\mathbb{G}),\quad\Rep(A^{\rtimes})\curvearrowleft\Rep(\mathbb{G}),\quad\Rep{G}(A)\curvearrowleft\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})),\quad\Rep(A^{\rtimes})\curvearrowleft\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})).

3.2.
From equivariant correspondence to module functor.

Fix
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
. If
\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\in\Rep{G}(B)=\mathrm{Corr}{G}(B,\mathbb{C})
, we have
\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H}\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,\mathbb{C})=\Rep{G}(A)
and if
s\in{}_{B}\mathscr{L}{G}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime})
, then we have the induced map

1\boxtimes_{B}s:\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\quad(1\boxtimes_{B}s)(y\otimes_{B}\xi)=y\otimes_{B}s\xi,\quad y\in\mathscr{L}_{B}(L^{2}(B),\mathcal{G}),\quad\xi\in\mathcal{H}.

Then
1\boxtimes_{B}s\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}{G}(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H},\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H}^{\prime})
. In this way, we obtain the normal
*
-functor

P(\mathcal{G})=F_{\mathcal{G}}:\Rep{G}(B)\to\Rep{G}(A).

Given
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep{G}(A)
and
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, we have unitary isomorphisms

S_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}:F_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})=\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\cong(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K}=F_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K}:y\otimes_{B}(\xi\otimes\eta)\mapsto(y\otimes_{B}\xi)\otimes\eta.

In this way, we can view
F_{\mathcal{G}}\in\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))
. Given
\mathcal{G},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
,
x\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}{G}_{B}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep{G}(B)
, we define

P(x)_{\mathcal{H}}=x\boxtimes_{B}1:F_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{H})=\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H}\to F_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{H})=\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H}:y\otimes_{B}\eta\mapsto xy\otimes_{B}\eta.

Then
P(x):=\{P(x)_{\mathcal{H}}\}_{\mathcal{H}\in\Rep{G}(B)}\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(F_{\mathcal{G}},F_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}})
. Combining all this, we find the functor

P:\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\to\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A)).

If
F\in\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))
, then
F(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}))\subseteq\Rep(A^{\rtimes})
(cfr.
[
DR25a
]
*Lemma 3.9). Thus, we have the following list of restriction functors:

\displaystyle(-)_{r}

\displaystyle:\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))\to\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes})),

\displaystyle(-)_{m}

\displaystyle:\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))\to\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A)),

\displaystyle(-)_{r,m}

\displaystyle:\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))\to\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes})).

The
r
-subscript means that we restrict a functor w.r.t. the inclusion
\Rep(B^{\rtimes})\subseteq\Rep{G}(B)
and the
m
-subscript means that we restrict the module action w.r.t. the inclusion
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))\subseteq\Rep(\mathbb{G})
. This leads to the following list of canonical functors:

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle:\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\to\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A)),

(3.1)

\displaystyle P_{r}=(-)_{r}\circ P

\displaystyle:\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\to\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes})),

(3.2)

\displaystyle P_{m}=(-)_{m}\circ P

\displaystyle:\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\to\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A)),

(3.3)

\displaystyle P_{r,m}=(-)_{r,m}\circ P

\displaystyle:\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\to\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes})).

(3.4)

3.3.
From module functor to equivariant correspondence

One of the main goals of this paper is to show that each of the functors (
3.1
), (
3.2
), (
3.3
), (
3.4
) defines an equivalence of categories. To do this, we will construct appropriate quasi-inverses for each of these functors. A first step in this direction was taken in
[
DR25a
]
*Theorem 3.5, Proposition 3.7, where the following result was proven:

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
F,G\in\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))
.

(1)

Consider
F((L^{2}(B),\pi_{B},U_{\beta}))=(\mathcal{G}_{F},\pi_{F},U_{F})\in\Rep{G}(A)
. There exists a unique unital, normal anti-
*
-representation
\rho_{F}:B\to B(\mathcal{G}_{F})
such that

\rho_{F}(b)\otimes 1=U_{F}S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}F((\rho_{B}\otimes R)\beta(b))S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}U_{F}^{*},\quad b\in B.

Then
(\mathcal{G}_{F},\pi_{F},\rho_{F},U_{F})\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
.

(2)

If
\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(F,G)
, then
\eta_{L^{2}(B)}\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}_{B}{G}(\mathcal{G}_{F},\mathcal{G}_{G}).

(3)

In this way, we obtain the functor

Q:\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))\to\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B).

(4)

We have
Q\circ P\cong\id_{\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)}
.

In
[
DR25a
]
*Theorem 4.5, it was proven that
Q
is quasi-inverse to
P
, if
\mathbb{G}
is a compact quantum group. We will prove that this is also true for general locally compact quantum groups in this paper. The proof in
[
DR25a
]
in the compact case makes use of the fact that
\Rep(A^{\rtimes})=\Rep{G}(A)
. However, if
\mathbb{G}
is non-compact, the inclusion
\Rep(A^{\rtimes})\subseteq\Rep{G}(A)
becomes strict, so we will have to do additional work to circumvent the issue.

It turns out that once we construct a quasi-inverse for (
3.4
), then finding quasi-inverses for (
3.1
), (
3.2
), (
3.3
) will follow relatively quickly. Thus, given
F\in\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes}))
, we want to find a way to associate a
\mathbb{G}
-
A
-
B
-correspondence to it. If we try to mimique Proposition
3.2
, one issue becomes apparent: if we consider
L^{2}(B)=(L^{2}(B),\pi_{B},U_{\beta})\in\Rep{G}(B)
, it may happen that
L^{2}(B)\notin\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
(this is already clear by taking
B=\mathbb{C}
and
\mathbb{G}
non-compact), so it does not make sense to evaluate the functor
F
in this object. It follows from
[
Va01
]
*Theorem 5.3 that a
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra
(B,\beta)
is
integrable
, in the sense that the canonical normal, faithful operator-valued weight
T_{\beta}:B^{+}\to\overline{B^{\beta}}^{+}

[
Va01
]
*Definition 3.1 is semi-finite, exactly when
L^{2}(B)\in\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
. For an integrable
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra
(B,\beta)
, we will now prove a version of Proposition
3.2
. Using the equivariant Morita theory, we will then be able to reduce the general case to the integrable case at a later stage.

Proposition 3.3
.

Suppose that
(B,\beta)
is integrable. Let
F,G\in\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes}))
.

(1)

Consider
F(L^{2}(B))=(\mathcal{G}_{F},\pi_{F},U_{F})\in\Rep(A^{\rtimes})\subseteq\Rep{G}(A)
. There exists a unique unital, normal anti-
*
-representation
\rho_{F}:B\to B(\mathcal{G}_{F})
such that

\rho_{F}(b)\otimes 1=U_{F}S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}F((\rho_{B}\otimes R)\beta(b))S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}U_{F}^{*},\quad b\in B.

Then
(\mathcal{G}_{F},\pi_{F},\rho_{F},U_{F})\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
.

(2)

If
\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(F,G)
, then
\eta_{L^{2}(B)}\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}_{B}{G}(\mathcal{G}_{F},\mathcal{G}_{G}).

(3)

In this way, we obtain the functor

Q_{r,m}:\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes}))\to\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B).

(4)

Q_{r,m}\circ P_{r,m}\cong\id_{\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)}.

Proof.

We will follow the same strategy as in
[
DR25a
]
. We give some details, since at one point, the proof has to be adapted because we are dealing with the smaller subcategory
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))\subseteq\Rep(\mathbb{G})
.

Recalling the notation introduced in (
2.4
), we have that
U_{F}\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}{G}(\mathcal{G}_{F}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),S{G}(\mathcal{G}_{F}))
is a unitary intertwiner. Consider then the unitary
\tilde{S}:F(S{G}(L^{2}(B)))\to S{G}(\mathcal{G}_{F})
defined by the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{S}:F(S{G}(L^{2}(B)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(L^{2}(B)\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}_{F}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S{G}(\mathcal{G}_{F}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(U_{\beta}^{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{F}}

Invoking (
2.5
), we have

\rho_{B^{\rtimes}}(B^{\rtimes})=\pi_{B^{\rtimes}}(B^{\rtimes})^{\prime}={}_{B}\mathscr{L}{G}(S{G}(L^{2}(B))).
We then endow the Hilbert space
\mathcal{G}_{F}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
with the
A^{\rtimes}
-
B^{\rtimes}
-correspondence structure given by:

•

the normal, unital
*
-representation
\pi_{F}^{\rtimes}:A^{\rtimes}\to B(\mathcal{G}_{F}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})):z\mapsto(\pi_{F}\otimes\id)(z)
,

•

the normal, unital, anti-
*
-representation
\tilde{\rho}_{F}:B^{\rtimes}\to B(\mathcal{G}_{F}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})):z\mapsto\tilde{S}F(\rho_{B^{\rtimes}}(z))\tilde{S}^{*}
.

Next, we introduce the following notations:

\displaystyle S^{2,2}_{V_{23}}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B)\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)},

\displaystyle S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{2,2}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B)\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)},

\displaystyle S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)},

\displaystyle S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)}.

It is important to note that all
\mathbb{G}
-representations occurring in the second factor of the above list of morphisms actually belong to
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
, and thus these morphisms make sense.
2
2

2

Comparing with the proof of
[
DR25a
]
*Theorem 3.5, this explains why there is an additional tensor factor
L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
, since it bypasses the fact that
(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\notin\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
if
\mathbb{G}
is not compact.
By the commutativity of the diagram (
2.7
), these morphisms are related by the following relations:

S^{1,3}_{V_{14}V_{34}}S^{2,2,*}_{V_{23}}=S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes 1\otimes 1=S^{1,3}_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}S^{2,2,*}_{V_{13}V_{23}}.

(3.5)

By Fell’s absorption principle (Proposition
2.1
), we have that
U:=(u{G}\otimes 1)V_{21}^{*}(u{G}\otimes 1)\in\mathscr{L}{G}(V_{13}V_{23},V_{13})
. Then also
U\otimes 1\in\mathscr{L}{G}(V_{14}V_{24}V_{34},V_{14}V_{34})
.
We then calculate

\displaystyle(1\otimes U\otimes 1)(S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}F((\rho_{B}\otimes R)(\beta(b)))S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(1\otimes U^{*}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=(1\otimes U\otimes 1)S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3}S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{2,2,*}(F((\rho_{B}\otimes R)\beta(b))\otimes 1\otimes 1)S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{2,2}S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}(1\otimes U^{*}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=(1\otimes U\otimes 1)S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3}F((\rho_{B}\otimes R)\beta(b)\otimes 1\otimes 1)S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}(1\otimes U^{*}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3}F(1\otimes U\otimes 1)F((\rho_{B}\otimes R)\beta(b)\otimes 1\otimes 1)F(1\otimes U^{*}\otimes 1)S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}

\displaystyle=S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3}F((\rho_{B}\otimes R)\beta(b)\otimes 1\otimes 1)S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}

\displaystyle=S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3}S_{V_{23}}^{2,2,*}(F((\rho_{B}\otimes R)\beta(b))\otimes 1\otimes 1)S_{V_{23}}^{2,2}S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}

\displaystyle=S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}F((\rho_{B}\otimes R)\beta(b))S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}\otimes 1\otimes 1.

Here, the first equality follows from (
3.5
), the second equality follows from naturality of
\{S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\}
in the first variable, the third equality follows from naturality of
\{S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\}
in the second variable, the fourth equality follows from the fact that
U\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime}\bar{\otimes}B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
, the fifth equality follows from naturality of
\{S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\}
in the first variable and the sixth equality follows from (
3.5
). Since the first leg of
U
generates the von Neumann algebra
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime}
, it follows from the above computation that

S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}F((\rho_{B}\otimes R)(\beta(b)))S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}\in B(\mathcal{G}_{F})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}).

Exactly as in the proof of
[
DR25a
]
*Theorem 3.5, we then see that
(\mathcal{G}_{F}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\pi_{F}^{\rtimes},\tilde{\rho}_{F},\check{V}_{23})\in\mathrm{Corr}^{\check{\mathbb{G}}}(A^{\rtimes},B^{\rtimes})
and
\tilde{\rho}_{F}(1\otimes\check{x})=U_{F}(1\otimes\rho_{\check{\mathbb{G}}}(\check{x}))U_{F}
for all
\check{x}\in L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})
. By
[
DR25a
]
*Lemma 3.6, it follows that there exists a unique unital, normal, anti-
*
-representation
\rho_{F}:B\to B(\mathcal{G}_{F})
such that
\tilde{\rho}_{F}=\rho_{F}^{\rtimes}
and
(\mathcal{G}_{F},\pi_{F},\rho_{F},U_{F})\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
. Thus,
(1)
is proven. The proof of
(2)
follows by the same computation as in
[
DR25a
]
*Proposition 3.7. The statement
(3)
follows immediately by
(1)
and
(2)
, and
(4)
follows from standard calculations.
∎

3.4.
Equivariant Eilenberg-Watts theorem

Proposition 3.4
.

Suppose that
(B,\beta)
is integrable. The functor

Q_{r,m}:\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes}))\to\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)

is fully faithful.

Proof.

Fix
F,G\in\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes}))
. We will prove that the map

\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(F,G)\to{}_{A}\mathscr{L}{G}_{B}(\mathcal{G}_{F},\mathcal{G}_{G}):\eta\mapsto\eta_{L^{2}(B)}

(3.6)

is bijective. The argument presented here follows the strategy of the proof of
[
DR25a
]
*Proposition 4.4, but we prefer to include most details for the convenience of the reader, since we will have to make small modifications.

If
\eta,\theta\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(F,G)
with
\eta_{L^{2}(B)}=\theta_{L^{2}(B)}
, then
\eta_{L^{2}(B)\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}=\theta_{L^{2}(B)\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}
. Since
L^{2}(B)\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
generates
\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
, it follows from the injectivity of (
2.1
) that
\eta=\theta
. Thus, the map (
3.6
) is injective. We now show that it is surjective as well.

Given
\eta_{1}\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}{G}_{B}(\mathcal{G}_{F},\mathcal{G}_{G})
, we must prove that there exists
\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(F,G)
such that
\eta_{L^{2}(B)}=\eta_{1}
. We define

\displaystyle\eta_{2}:=S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})},

\displaystyle\eta_{3}:=S_{L^{2}(B)\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}(\eta_{2}\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B)\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}=S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}.

Exactly as in the proof of
[
DR25a
]
*Proposition 4.4, we then see that

\eta_{2}F((\rho_{B}\otimes R)\beta(b))=G((\rho_{B}\otimes R)\beta(b))\eta_{2},\quad b\in B.

We now claim that

\eta_{3}F(x)=G(x)\eta_{3},\quad x\in{}_{B^{\rtimes}}\mathscr{L}(L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))),

(3.7)

which as in
[
DR25a
]
*Proposition 4.4 boils down to showing the commutation relation

\eta_{3}F(V_{23}^{*}U_{\beta,13}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1\otimes 1)U_{\beta,13}V_{23})=G(V_{23}^{*}U_{\beta,13}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1\otimes 1)U_{\beta,13}V_{23})\eta_{3},\quad b\in B.

(3.8)

The proof of this commutation relation in
[
DR25a
]
*Proposition 4.4 no longer works in the non-compact case, since
(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\notin\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
. To remedy this, we introduce an extra tensor factor, although for the rest the argument is mostly the same, but we prefer to include the lengthy calculation for the convenience of the reader.

As before, we will simplify some notation. Therefore, we write

\displaystyle S_{V}^{1,1}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)},

\displaystyle S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{1,2}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)},

\displaystyle S_{V_{23}}^{2,2}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)},

\displaystyle S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)},

\displaystyle S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)},

\displaystyle S_{V}^{3,1}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B)\lhd((L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)}.

By Fell’s absorption principle (Proposition
2.1
), we have

V^{*}\Sigma\in\mathscr{L}{G}((L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{13}),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{13}V_{23})).

We then compute

\displaystyle(\eta_{3}\otimes 1)(F(V_{23}^{*}U_{\beta,13}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})U_{\beta,13}V_{23})\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=(S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{1,2,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 3})(S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{1,2}\otimes 1)(F(V_{23}^{*}U_{\beta,13}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})U_{\beta,13}V_{23})\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=(S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{1,2,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 3})S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3}S_{V}^{3,1,*}(F(V_{23}^{*}U_{\beta,13}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})U_{\beta,13}V_{23})\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=(S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{1,2,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 3})S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3}F(V_{23}^{*}U_{\beta,13}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1^{\otimes 3})U_{\beta,13}V_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=(S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{1,2,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 3})S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3}F((V^{*}\Sigma)_{23})F(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})F((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=(S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{1,2,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 3})(V^{*}\Sigma)_{23}S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3}F(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})F((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=(S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{1,2,*}\otimes 1)(V^{*}\Sigma)_{23}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 3})S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3}F(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})F((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=(S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{1,2,*}\otimes 1)(V^{*}\Sigma)_{23}(S_{V}^{1,1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})(\eta_{2}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})(S_{V}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3}F(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})F((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=(S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{1,2,*}\otimes 1)(V^{*}\Sigma)_{23}(S_{V}^{1,1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})(\eta_{2}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})S^{2,2}_{V_{23}}F(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})F((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=(S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{1,2,*}\otimes 1)(V^{*}\Sigma)_{23}(S_{V}^{1,1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})(\eta_{2}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})(F(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta})\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})S^{2,2}_{V_{23}}F((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=(S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{1,2,*}\otimes 1)(V^{*}\Sigma)_{23}(S_{V}^{1,1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})(G(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta})\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})(\eta_{2}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})S^{2,2}_{V_{23}}F((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=S_{V}^{3,1}S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}(V^{*}\Sigma)_{23}(S_{V}^{1,1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})(G(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta})\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})(\eta_{2}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})S_{V_{23}}^{2,2}F((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=S_{V}^{3,1}G((V^{*}\Sigma)_{23})S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}(S_{V}^{1,1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})(G(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta})\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})(\eta_{2}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})S_{V_{23}}^{2,2}F((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=S_{V}^{3,1}G((V^{*}\Sigma)_{23})S_{V_{23}}^{2,2,*}(G(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta})\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})(\eta_{2}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})S_{V_{23}}^{2,2}F((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=S_{V}^{3,1}G((V^{*}\Sigma)_{23})G(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})S_{V_{23}}^{2,2,*}(\eta_{2}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})S_{V_{23}}^{2,2}F((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=S_{V}^{3,1}G((V^{*}\Sigma)_{23})G(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})S_{V_{23}}^{2,2,*}(S_{V}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})(\eta_{1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 3})(S_{V}^{1,1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})S_{V_{23}}^{2,2}F((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=S_{V}^{3,1}G((V^{*}\Sigma)_{23})G(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 3})S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3}F((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=S_{V}^{3,1}G((V^{*}\Sigma)_{23})G(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 3})(\Sigma V)_{23}S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3}S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=S_{V}^{3,1}G((V^{*}\Sigma)_{23})G(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}(\Sigma V)_{23}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 3})S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3}S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=S_{V}^{3,1}G((V^{*}\Sigma)_{23})G(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})G((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 3})S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3}S_{V}^{3,1,*}

\displaystyle=S_{V}^{3,1}G((V^{*}\Sigma)_{23})G(U_{\beta}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta}\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})G((\Sigma V)_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}(S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{1,2,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1^{\otimes 3})(S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{1,2}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=S_{V}^{3,1}G(V_{23}^{*}U_{\beta,13}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1^{\otimes 3})U_{\beta,13}V_{23})S_{V}^{3,1,*}(\eta_{3}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=(G(V_{23}^{*}U_{\beta,13}^{*}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1^{\otimes 2})U_{\beta,13}V_{23})\otimes 1)(\eta_{3}\otimes 1),

and (
3.8
) follows.

Since
L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
is a generator for
\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
, it follows from (
3.7
) and the bijectivity of (
2.1
) that there exists a unique natural transformation
\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}(F,G)
such that
\eta_{3}=\eta_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}
.

By Fell’s absorption principle (Proposition
2.1
), we may pick an isometric intertwiner

T:(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\to(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V).

The calculation

\displaystyle G(1\otimes T)\eta_{L^{2}(B)\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}

\displaystyle=\eta_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}F(1\otimes T)

\displaystyle=S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}F(1\otimes T)

\displaystyle=S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}(1\otimes T)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}

\displaystyle=G(1\otimes T)S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}=G(1\otimes T)\eta_{2},

combined with the fact that
T
is isometric ensures that
\eta_{L^{2}(B)\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}=\eta_{2}
.

Given
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
and
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
, we now prove that the rectangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes 1}

(3.9)

commutes.
By construction, (
3.9
) commutes if
\mathcal{H}=L^{2}(B)\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
and
\mathcal{K}=L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
. Both compositions
F(\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\to G(\mathcal{H})\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
in (
3.9
) define natural transformations
F(-\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\implies G(-)\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
, and they agree in the generator
L^{2}(B)\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\in\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
. By the injectivity of (
2.1
), it thus follows that (
3.9
) commutes when
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
is arbitrary and
\mathcal{K}=L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
. Similarly, fixing
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
, we obtain natural transformations
F(\mathcal{H}\lhd-)\implies G(\mathcal{H})\lhd-
that agree on the generator
L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
, so that by the injectivity of (
2.1
) we find that (
3.9
) commutes for all
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
and all
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
.

In conclusion, we find that
\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(F,G)
. By the definition of
\eta_{2}
, we then also find
\eta_{L^{2}(B)}=\eta_{1}
, and the surjectivity of (
3.6
) is proven.
∎

We emphasize that in the following results, integrability of
(B,\beta)
no longer plays a role.

Theorem 3.5
.

The restriction functors

\displaystyle\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))\to\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))

(3.10)

\displaystyle\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes}))\to\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes}))

(3.11)

are isomorphisms of categories.

Proof.

Let either
\mathscr{C}_{A}=\Rep{G}(A)
and
\mathscr{C}_{B}=\Rep{G}(B)
or
\mathscr{C}_{A}=\Rep(A^{\rtimes})
and
\mathscr{C}_{A}=\Rep{G}(A)
.

Let a functor
F\in\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\mathscr{C}_{B},\mathscr{C}_{A})
be given. Implicitly, this means we have natural unitaries

S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}:F(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\to F(\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K},\quad\mathcal{H}\in\mathscr{C}_{B},\quad\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))

satisfying the compatibilities (
2.7
).

Fix for the moment
\mathcal{H}\in\mathscr{C}_{B}
and
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
. We write

Y:=S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\circ S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}:F(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\to(F(\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}).

By Fell’s absorption principle (Proposition
2.1
),
X:=\Sigma V\in\mathscr{L}{G}(V_{13}V_{23},V_{13})
. We then calculate

\displaystyle(1\otimes 1\otimes X\otimes 1)(Y\otimes 1\otimes 1)(1\otimes 1\otimes X^{*}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=(1\otimes 1\otimes X\otimes 1)S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}(S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(1\otimes 1\otimes X^{*}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=(1\otimes 1\otimes X\otimes 1)S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}(1\otimes 1\otimes X^{*}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}F(1\otimes 1\otimes X\otimes 1)F(1\otimes 1\otimes X^{*}\otimes 1)S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}

\displaystyle=S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}(S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}\otimes 1\otimes 1)=Y\otimes 1\otimes 1.

We view
Y\in B(F(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}),F(\mathcal{H})\otimes\mathcal{K})\bar{\otimes}B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
as having two tensor legs. Then it follows from the above computation that

V_{23}Y_{12}V_{23}^{*}=Y_{13}
. In particular,
Y
has its second leg inside
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
. On the other hand,
Y_{13}=(\id\otimes\Delta)(Y)=W_{23}^{*}Y_{13}W_{23}
so
Y
also has its second leg in
L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})
. Since
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\cap L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})=\mathbb{C}1
, it follows that there exists a unique unitary
S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}:F(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\to F(\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K}
such that

S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}=(S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1)\circ S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}.

(3.12)

The notation is not ambiguous, because it coincides with the already defined operator
S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}
in case that
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
, as follows from the commutativity of the diagram (
2.7
).
It is straightforward to verify that the unitaries
\{S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\}_{\mathcal{H}\in\mathscr{C}_{B},\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})}
are natural in both variables.

Our next goal is to show that the commutation relation (
2.7
) is satisfied for the unitaries
\{S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\}_{\mathcal{H}\in\mathscr{C}_{B},\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})}
. In other words, given
\mathcal{H}\in\mathscr{C}_{B}
and
\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, we will prove commutativity of the triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F((\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime})=F(\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F(\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}=F(\mathcal{H})\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1}

(3.13)

By Fell’s absorption principle (Proposition
2.1
), we may pick a unitary
T\in\mathscr{L}{G}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime})
. We then consider the diagram
