{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}))\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd(\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H})\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H})\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\ }
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes 1\otimes T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes 1\otimes T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})),\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}}

(3.14)

The commutativity of (
3.13
) will follow once we can show the commutativity of the small triangle immediately above the middle rectangle in the diagram (
3.14
).

First, we note that the two small most upper triangles commute by (
3.12
). Next, we note that the outer triangle appearing above the central rectangle commutes by (
2.7
), under the additional assumption that
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
(this uses the fact that
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})
). By a diagram chase, we then see that the small triangle immediately above the middle rectangle commutes, provided that
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
. Consequently, (
3.13
) commutes when
\mathcal{H}\in\mathscr{C}_{B}
,
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
and
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
. Now, we return to the general case where
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, and we shift our attention to the other parts of the diagram (
3.14
) as well. The triangle appearing immediately below the middle rectangle commutes by (
3.12
). The lower left and lower right triangles commute since we already argued that (
3.13
) commutes when
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
. Furthermore, the middle rectangle trivially commutes, and the outer hexagon commutes: both compositions equal
F(1\otimes 1\otimes T)
, as follows from the naturality of
\{S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\}_{\mathcal{H}\in\mathscr{C}_{B},\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}
in the second variable. By a diagram chase, we then conclude the commutativity of the small triangle immediately above the middle rectangle, and the commutativity of (
3.13
) follows.

Thus, we have proven that every
F\in\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\mathscr{C}_{B},\mathscr{C}_{A})
can be uniquely extended to a functor
F\in\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\mathscr{C}_{B},\mathscr{C}_{A})
.

Next, let
F,G\in\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\mathscr{C}_{B},\mathscr{C}_{A})
be given. We will now prove that

\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(F,G)=\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(F,G).

For this, we fix
\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(F,G)
,
\mathcal{H}\in\mathscr{C}_{B}
and
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
. We also consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H})\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H})\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes 1\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}}

The most left and most right part of the diagram clearly commute, as does the lower rectangle, by the fact that
\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(F,G)
and since
\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
. Furthermore, the outer part of the diagram also commutes, since
\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(F,G)
. By another diagram chase, we see that the upper rectangle commutes. As such, we deduce that
\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(F,G)
.
∎

Proposition 3.6
.

The restriction functors

\displaystyle\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))\to\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes})),

(3.15)

\displaystyle\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))\to\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes}))

(3.16)

are fully faithful.

Proof.

Since the functor (
3.16
) is the composition of the functors (
3.10
) and (
3.15
), it suffices to show that the functor (
3.15
) is fully faithful. In other words, given
F,G\in\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))
, we will prove that the map

\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(F,G)\to\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(F,G):(\eta_{\mathcal{K}})_{\mathcal{K}\in\Rep{G}(B)}\mapsto(\eta_{\mathcal{K}})_{\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(B^{\rtimes})}

(3.17)

is bijective.

If
(\eta_{\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{H}\in\Rep{G}(B)},(\theta_{\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{H}\in\Rep{G}(B)}
agree on
\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
, then for
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep{G}(B)
, we have
\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\in\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
, and thus

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes 1

\displaystyle=S_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\circ\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\circ S_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}=S_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\circ\theta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\circ S_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}=\theta_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes 1.

Therefore,
(\eta_{\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{H}\in\Rep{G}(B)},(\theta_{\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{H}\in\Rep{G}(B)}
agree everywhere, showing the injectivity of (
3.17
).

To show the surjectivity of (
3.17
), we argue similarly as in the proof of Theorem
3.5
. Let
(\eta_{\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B^{\rtimes})}\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(F,G)
be given. Given
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep{G}(B)
, we have
\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\in\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
and it makes sense to consider the composition

Y:=S_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\circ\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\circ S_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}:F(\mathcal{H})\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\to G(\mathcal{H})\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}).

Writing

X:=\Sigma V\in\mathscr{L}{G}((L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I}))

and introducing the notations

\displaystyle S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3}

\displaystyle:=S_{\mathcal{H},(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)},

\displaystyle S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3}

\displaystyle:=S_{\mathcal{H},(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)},

\displaystyle S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{2,2}

\displaystyle:=S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)},

\displaystyle S_{V_{23}}^{2,2}

\displaystyle:=S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)},

we compute

\displaystyle(1\otimes X\otimes 1)(Y\otimes 1\otimes 1)(1\otimes X^{*}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=(1\otimes X\otimes 1)S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3}S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{2,2,*}(\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes 1\otimes 1)S_{V_{13}V_{23}}^{2,2}S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}(1\otimes X^{*}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=(1\otimes X\otimes 1)S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3}\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}S_{V_{14}V_{24}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}(1\otimes X^{*}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3}G(1\otimes X\otimes 1)\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}F(1\otimes X^{*}\otimes 1)S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}

\displaystyle=S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3}\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd((L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V))}S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3,*}

\displaystyle=S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{1,3}S_{V_{23}}^{2,2,*}(\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes 1\otimes 1)S_{V_{23}}^{2,2}S_{V_{14}V_{34}}^{3,1,*}=Y\otimes 1\otimes 1,

from which it follows that

(1\otimes V)(Y\otimes 1)(1\otimes V^{*})=Y_{13}.
Consequently, we see that
Y\in B(F(\mathcal{H}),G(\mathcal{H}))\bar{\otimes}\mathbb{C}1
, so there exists a unique bounded linear operator
\eta_{\mathcal{H}}:F(\mathcal{H})\to G(\mathcal{H})
such that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H})\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H})\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}}

(3.18)

commutes. Again, the notation
\eta_{\mathcal{H}}
is unambiguous and it is straightforward to verify that
(\eta_{\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{H}\in\Rep{G}(B)}
defines a natural transformation.

Given
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep{G}(B)
and
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
, we now prove that the rectangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes 1}

(3.19)

commutes. To do this, we fix a unitary
T\in\mathscr{L}{G}(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop\mathcal{K})
and we consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop\mathcal{K}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H}\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop\mathcal{K}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\lhd\mathcal{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\lhd\mathcal{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H})\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop\mathcal{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H})\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop\mathcal{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{H})\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{H})\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(1\otimes T)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(1\otimes T)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop\mathcal{K})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathcal{K}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathcal{K}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes T^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes 1\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes T^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes 1\otimes 1}

Consider the five subrectangles stacked on top of each other. Numbering from top to bottom, we explain why each of them commutes. The first one commutes since
\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
, the second one by naturality of
\eta
, the third one since
\mathcal{H}\lhd L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\in\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
, the fourth one by (
3.18
) and the fifth one commutes trivially. Furthermore, it is easy to see that the most left and the most right part of the diagram commute. Consequently, the entire outer part of the diagram commutes. By a diagram chase, it follows that the rectangle (
3.19
) commutes.
∎

The following useful result will allow us to transfer results established from the integrable case to the general case:

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
(B,\beta)
be a
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra. There exists an integrable
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra
(C,\gamma)
that is
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-Morita equivalent with
(B,\beta)
.

Proof.

If
(A,\check{\check{\alpha}})
is an integrable
\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}}
-
W^{*}
-algebra, then it is easily verified that
(A,\alpha)
is an integrable
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra, where

\alpha(a):=(1\otimes u{G})\check{\check{\alpha}}(a)(1\otimes u{G}),\quad a\in A.

It follows from
[
Va01
]
*Proposition 2.5 that the
\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}}
-
W^{*}
-algebra
(B^{\rtimes\rtimes},\beta^{\rtimes\rtimes})
is integrable. Consequently, writing
C:=B^{\rtimes\rtimes}
and considering

\gamma(c):=(1\otimes u{G})\beta^{\rtimes\rtimes}(c)(1\otimes u{G}),\quad c\in C,

it follows that the
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra
(C,\gamma)
is integrable. The von Neumann algebra
B\bar{\otimes}B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
carries the
\mathbb{G}
-action

\delta(z):=W_{32}(\beta\otimes\id)(z)_{132}W_{32}^{*},\quad z\in B\bar{\otimes}B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})),

and the Takesaki-Takai isomorphism

\Phi:(B\bar{\otimes}B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})),\delta)\to(C,\gamma):z\mapsto V_{23}^{*}(\beta\otimes\id)(z)V_{23}

is
\mathbb{G}
-equivariant. It is then easily verified that we have
(B,\beta)\sim{G}(C,\gamma)
, with an explicit
\mathbb{G}
-
C
-
B
-Morita correspondence given by the Hilbert space
L^{2}(B)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
endowed with the
C
-
B
-bimodule structure

c\xi b:=(\pi_{B}\otimes\id)(\Phi^{-1}(c))(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)\xi,\quad\xi\in L^{2}(B)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\quad b\in B,\quad c\in C

and the unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation

W_{32}U_{\beta,13}\in B(L^{2}(B)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
, cfr.
[
DCDR24
]
*Theorem 5.25.
∎

We then come to the first main result of this paper:

Theorem 3.8

(Equivariant Eilenberg-Watts theorem I)
.

The functors (
3.1
), (
3.2
), (
3.3
) and (
3.4
) are all equivalences of categories. Moreover,
P
and
Q
are quasi-inverse to each other.

Proof.

We start by showing that the functor (
3.4
) is an equivalence of categories. To emphasize the dependency on the
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebras
A,B
, let us write

P_{r,m}=P_{r,m}^{A,B}:\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\to\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes})).

By Lemma
3.7
, we can fix an integrable
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra
(C,\gamma)
together with a
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-Morita correspondence
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(B,C)
. Considering the dual
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-Morita correspondence
\overline{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(C,B)

[
DCDR24
]
*Definition 5.1, we have unitary isomorphisms
\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{C}\overline{\mathcal{G}}\cong L^{2}(B)
and
\overline{\mathcal{G}}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{G}\cong L^{2}(C)
of equivariant correspondences
[
DCDR24
]
*Proposition 5.10. One then verifies that

F_{\mathcal{G}}\in\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(C^{\rtimes}),\Rep(B^{\rtimes})),\quad F_{\overline{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(C^{\rtimes}))

implement equivalences of
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
-module categories. Therefore, we obtain the induced equivalences

\displaystyle S:\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\to\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,C):\mathcal{K}\mapsto\mathcal{K}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{G},

\displaystyle T:\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(C^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes}))\to\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes})):F\mapsto F\circ F_{\overline{\mathcal{G}}}

of categories. We then verify that

T\circ P_{r,m}^{A,C}\circ S\cong P_{r,m}^{A,B}
,
so we can assume without loss of generality that
(B,\beta)
is integrable. In that case, it suffices to show that the functor

Q_{r,m}:\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes}))\to\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)

from Proposition
3.3
is quasi-inverse to
P_{r,m}
. This follows from Proposition
3.4
and from the fact that
Q_{r,m}\circ P_{r,m}\cong\id_{\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)}
.

Next, we show that
P:\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\to\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))
is an equivalence of categories. First, we prove this if
(B,\beta)
is integrable. In that case, we have the factorization

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{r,m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q_{r,m}}

Since both
Q_{r,m}
and
(-)_{r,m}
are fully faithful (Proposition
3.6
), it follows that
Q
is fully faithful as well. Since
Q\circ P\cong\id_{\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)}
, it thus follows that
P
is an equivalence of categories. By a similar Morita argument as before, it follows that
P
is an equivalence of categories in the non-integrable case as well. Since we have
Q\circ P\cong\id_{\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)}
, it follows that
Q
is quasi-inverse to
P
.

Keeping in mind the factorizations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep(B^{\rtimes}),\Rep(A^{\rtimes}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{r,m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Fun_{\Rep(\mathbb{G})}(\Rep{G}(B),\Rep{G}(A))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

where the isomorphisms come from Theorem
3.5
, it follows that
P_{r}
and
P_{m}
are equivalences as well.
∎

Corollary 3.9
.

The restriction functors (
3.15
) and (
3.16
) are equivalences of categories.

We can also extend
[
DR25a
]
*Theorem 3.10.
The key idea behind the next result is that under the equivalences (
3.1
), (
3.2
), (
3.3
) and (
3.4
), an equivalence of the appropriate module categories corresponds exactly with a
\mathbb{G}
-
A
-
B
-Morita correspondence.

Proposition 3.10
.

The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

(A,\alpha)\sim{G}(B,\beta)
.

(2)

There exist
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
and unitary isomorphisms
\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\overline{\mathcal{G}}\cong L^{2}(A)
and
\overline{\mathcal{G}}\boxtimes_{A}\mathcal{G}\cong L^{2}(B)
of equivariant correspondences.

(3)

\Rep{G}(A)
and
\Rep{G}(B)
are equivalent as
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
-
W^{*}
-module categories.

(4)

\Rep{G}(A)
and
\Rep{G}(B)
are equivalent as
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
-
W^{*}
-module categories.

(5)

\Rep(A^{\rtimes})
and
\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
are equivalent as
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
-
W^{*}
-module categories.

(6)

\Rep(A^{\rtimes})
and
\Rep(B^{\rtimes})
are equivalent as
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
-
W^{*}
-module categories.

Proof.

The implication
(1)\implies(2)
was established in
[
DCDR24
]
*Proposition 5.10. The implication
(2)\implies(3)
follows by considering the normal
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
-module
*
-functors

F_{\mathcal{G}}:\Rep{G}(B)\to\Rep{G}(A),\quad F_{\overline{\mathcal{G}}}:\Rep{G}(A)\to\Rep{G}(B).

The implications
(3)\implies(4)\implies(6)
and
(3)\implies(5)\implies(6)
are trivial. It remains to prove
(6)\implies(1)
.
Assume that
(6)
holds and choose an equivalence

F:\Rep(B^{\rtimes})\to\Rep(A^{\rtimes})
of
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
-
W^{*}
-module categories. By Lemma
3.7
, we may assume that
(B,\beta)
is integrable. Consider the associated
\mathbb{G}
-
A
-
B
-correspondence
\mathcal{G}_{F}=F(L^{2}(B))
constructed in Proposition
3.3
. It then follows exactly as in the proof of
[
DR25a
]
*Theorem 3.10 that
\mathcal{G}_{F}
is a
\mathbb{G}
-
A
-
B
-Morita correspondence.
∎

4.
Equivariant Eilenberg-Watts theorem -
\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
-module version

Throughout this section, we fix a locally compact quantum group
\mathbb{G}
and two (right)
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebras
(A,\alpha)
and
(B,\beta)
.

4.1.
Universal quantum groups.

This section will rely crucially on tools developed in the paper
[
Kus01
]
, where the lifted versions

\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!\in M(C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C^{*}(\mathbb{G})),\quad\mathds{W}\in M(C^{*}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})),\quad{\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}\in M(C^{*}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))

of the multiplicative unitary
W\in M(C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}))
are introduced, their basic properties are established and the connection with the representation theory of
\mathbb{G}
is clarified. However, since we work with ‘right’ conventions, it will be convenient to work with the lifted versions

\mathds{V}\in M(C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G})),\quad\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!\in M(C_{0}^{r}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\otimes C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})),\quad\hbox to8.58pt{\vbox to7.44pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.52724pt\lower-6.99443pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -0.45 0 L 4.53 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.89 0 L 10.87 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.91 0 L 5.21 -9.06}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.51 0 L 5.21 -9.06}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.17 0 L 5.21 -5.89}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.24 0 L 5.21 -5.89}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\in M(C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))

of the multiplicative unitary
V\in M(C_{0}^{r}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}))
instead. We are not aware of a full explicit account of these lifted versions in the literature, so we give some details surrounding them.

It follows from
[
Kus01
]
*Proposition 4.2 that there exists a unique element
\mathds{V}\in M(C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}))
such that
(\id\otimes\omega)(\mathds{V})=\omega
for all
\omega\in L_{\sharp}^{1}(\mathbb{G})
. Moreover,
\mathds{V}
is a unitary satisfying
(\id\otimes\Delta)(\mathds{V})=\mathds{V}_{12}\mathds{V}_{13}
. In fact, we can simply
define

\mathds{V}:=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{21}
.

Fix a
C^{*}
-algebra
C
. By a unitary corepresentation of
(C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}),\Delta)
on
C
, we mean a unitary
U\in M(C\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}))
such that
(\id\otimes\Delta)(U)=U_{12}U_{13}
. Given a unitary corepresentation
U\in M(C\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}))
, the map

\phi_{U}:L_{\sharp}^{1}(\mathbb{G})\to M(C):\omega\mapsto(\id\otimes\omega)(U)

is a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism
[
Kus01
]
*Proposition 5.2, so it extends uniquely to a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism
\phi_{U}:C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\to M(C)
. Conversely, if
\phi:C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\to M(C)
is a non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism, then
U_{\phi}:=(\phi\otimes\id)(\mathds{V})\in M(C\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}))
is a unitary corepresentation on
C
. The assignments
U\mapsto\phi_{U}
and
\phi\mapsto U_{\phi}
are inverse to each other, so the unitary corepresentations on
C
are in bijective correspondence with the non-degenerate
*
-homomorphisms
C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\to M(C)
.

As in
[
Kus01
]
*Proposition 6.1, one uses this bijection to introduce the comultiplication

\Delta_{C^{*}(\mathbb{G})}:C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\to M(C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C^{*}(\mathbb{G}))

uniquely determined by
(\Delta_{C^{*}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\id)(\mathds{V})=\mathds{V}_{13}\mathds{V}_{23}
. Given unitary corepresentations
U_{C}\in M(C\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}))
and
U_{D}\in M(D\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}))
, we also have that
U_{C,13}U_{D,23}\in M(C\otimes D\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}))
is a unitary corepresentation, and

(\phi_{U_{C}}\otimes\phi_{U_{D}})\circ\Delta_{C^{*}(\mathbb{G})}=\phi_{U_{C,13}U_{D,23}}.

Let us write
\check{\mathds{V}}\in M(C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
for the version of
\mathds{V}
associated to the locally compact quantum group
\check{\mathbb{G}}
. We then define
\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!:=\check{\mathds{V}}_{21}\in M(C_{0}^{r}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\otimes C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
and it satisfies the identities

(\check{\Delta}\otimes\id)(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!)=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!_{13}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!_{23},\quad(\id\otimes\Delta_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!)=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!_{12}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!_{13}.

We also consider the surjective
*
-homomorphisms (note that
V_{21}
defines a unitary
\check{\mathbb{G}}
-representation)

\displaystyle\pi{G}:C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\to C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}):L_{\sharp}^{1}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\ni\check{\omega}\mapsto(\id\otimes\check{\omega})(V_{21})=(\check{\omega}\otimes\id)(V),

\displaystyle\pi_{\check{\mathbb{G}}}:C^{*}(\mathbb{G})\to C_{0}^{r}(\check{\mathbb{G}}):L_{\sharp}^{1}(\mathbb{G})\ni\omega\mapsto(\id\otimes\omega)(V).

They satisfy
(\pi_{\check{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\id)(\mathds{V})=V=(\id\otimes\pi{G})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!)
, and we have the following relations (cfr.
[
Kus01
]
*Proposition 6.2):

\displaystyle\mathds{V}^{*}(1\otimes\pi_{\check{\mathbb{G}}}(x))\mathds{V}

\displaystyle=(\id\otimes\pi_{\check{\mathbb{G}}})\Delta_{C^{*}(\mathbb{G})}(x),\quad x\in C^{*}(\mathbb{G}),

(4.1)

\displaystyle\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!(\pi{G}(x)\otimes 1)\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!^{*}

\displaystyle=(\pi{G}\otimes\id)\Delta_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(x),\quad x\in C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}}).

(4.2)

Using these two relations, we argue as in
[
Kus01
]
*Proposition 6.4 that there exists a unique unitary

, the element

is a unitary corepresentation of the

-bialgebra
(C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}}),\Delta_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})})
and it satisfies
(\id\otimes\pi{G})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!)=U
.

and

with associated non-degenerate

\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!^{\mathcal{K}}:=(\id\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{K}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!)=(\phi_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{K}})(\hbox to8.58pt{\vbox to7.44pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.52724pt\lower-6.99443pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{
