For example, given a
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra
(A,\alpha)
and
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
, we have the associated unitary implementation
U_{\alpha}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
and we will write
\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\alpha}^{\mathcal{K}}=(\id\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{K}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\alpha})\in B(L^{2}(A)\otimes\mathcal{K})
.

4.2.
Module category associated to dynamical system.

Let
(A,\alpha)
be a right
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra. As in
[
DCK24
]
*Section 4, there exists a unique unital normal injective
*
-homomorphism
\alpha^{u}:A\to A\bar{\otimes}C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}
such that for every
(\mathcal{H},\pi,U)\in\Rep{G}(A)=\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,\mathbb{C})
(where
\pi
is not necessarily faithful), we have

(\pi\otimes\id)(\alpha^{u}(a))=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!(\pi(a)\otimes 1)\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!^{*},\quad a\in A,

(4.3)

where we view
\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!\in M(\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})\otimes C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))\subseteq B(\mathcal{H})\bar{\otimes}C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}
.

On the other hand, considering the universal version
R_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})}:C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\to C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})
of the unitary antipode uniquely determined by
(\check{R}\otimes R_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!)=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!
(cfr.
[
Kus01
]
*Proposition 7.2), and considering its unique normal extension
\tilde{R}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})}:C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}\to C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}
, it follows similarly as in
[
DCK24
]
*Lemma 4.6 that for every
(\mathcal{H},\rho,U)\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(\mathbb{C},B)
, we have that

(\rho\otimes\tilde{R}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})})(\beta^{u}(b))=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!^{*}(\rho(b)\otimes 1)\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!,\quad b\in B.

(4.4)

Note also that (cfr.
[
DCK24
]
*Lemma 4.7)

(\alpha\otimes\id)\circ\alpha^{u}=(\id\otimes\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}})\circ\alpha,

(4.5)

where

\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}:L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\to L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}
is given by
\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}(x)=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!(x\otimes 1)\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!^{*}
.

By the universal property of the bidual, the non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism

\Delta_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})}:C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\to M(C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\otimes C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))

can be uniquely extended to a unital normal
*
-homomorphism

\tilde{\Delta}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})}:C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}\to C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}\bar{\otimes}C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}.

Using this observation, we can make sense of the coaction condition for the lifted coaction
\alpha^{u}:

Lemma 4.1
.

The rectangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\bar{\otimes}C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\bar{\otimes}C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\bar{\otimes}C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}\bar{\otimes}C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{u}\otimes\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})}}

commutes.

Proof.

Let
\tilde{\pi}{G}:C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}\to L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
be the unique unital, normal
*
-homomorphism extending
\pi{G}:C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\to C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G})
. We then note that we have the commutation relation

\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}\circ\tilde{\pi}{G}=(\tilde{\pi}{G}\otimes\id)\circ\tilde{\Delta}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})}.

Indeed, rewriting (
4.2
), we see that

\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}(\pi{G}(x))=(\pi{G}\otimes\id)\Delta_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(x)
for
x\in C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})
.
The desired commutation relation then follows
by
\sigma
-weak density of
C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})
inside
C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}
. Consequently, we can calculate for
x\in C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}
that

\displaystyle(\id\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})})\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}(\tilde{\pi}{G}(x))

\displaystyle=(\id\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})})(\tilde{\pi}{G}\otimes\id)\tilde{\Delta}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(x)

\displaystyle=(\tilde{\pi}{G}\otimes\id\otimes\id)(\tilde{\Delta}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})}\otimes\id)\tilde{\Delta}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(x)

\displaystyle=(\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}\otimes\id)(\tilde{\pi}{G}\otimes\id)\tilde{\Delta}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(x)

\displaystyle=(\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}\otimes\id)\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}(\tilde{\pi}{G}(x)),

so it follows that

(\id\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})})\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}=(\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}\otimes\id)\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}.

As a result, making use of (
4.5
), we find

\displaystyle(\alpha\otimes\id\otimes\id)(\alpha^{u}\otimes\id)\alpha^{u}

\displaystyle=(\id\otimes\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}\otimes\id)(\alpha\otimes\id)\alpha^{u}

\displaystyle=(\id\otimes\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}\otimes\id)(\id\otimes\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}})\alpha

\displaystyle=(\id\otimes\id\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})})(\id\otimes\Delta^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}})\alpha

\displaystyle=(\id\otimes\id\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})})(\alpha\otimes\id)\alpha^{u}

\displaystyle=(\alpha\otimes\id\otimes\id)(\id\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})})\alpha^{u}.

Since
\alpha\otimes\id\otimes\id
is injective, we conclude that
(\alpha^{u}\otimes\id)\alpha^{u}=(\id\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})})\alpha^{u}
.
∎

We can now define the
W^{*}
-module category
\Rep(A)\curvearrowleft\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
. Given
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
, consider the associated non-degenerate
*
-representation
\phi_{\mathcal{K}}:C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\to B(\mathcal{K})
. By the universal property of the bidual, it extends uniquely to a normal, unital
*
-representation
\tilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{K}}:C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}\to B(\mathcal{K})
. If also
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(A)
, we define the normal, unital
*
-representation

\pi_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}}:A\to B(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K}):a\mapsto(\pi_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\tilde{\phi}_{\mathcal{K}})(\alpha^{u}(a)).

We then obtain the object
\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}:=(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K},\pi_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}})\in\Rep(A)
. Moreover, it follows from Lemma
4.1
that

\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime})=(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\quad\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(A),\quad\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\in\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}}).

If
\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\in\Rep(A)
and
\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\in\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
,
x\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime})
and
y\in\mathscr{L}^{\check{\mathbb{G}}}(\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime})
, it is easily verified that
x\lhd y:=x\otimes y\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime})
. Consequently, we obtain
the
W^{*}
-module category
\Rep(A)\curvearrowleft\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
.

By restriction, we then also obtain the
W^{*}
-module category
\Rep(A)\curvearrowleft\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}})).

Remark 4.2
.

Let
H
be a bialgebra and let
(A,\alpha)
be a (right)
H
-comodule algebra, i.e.
A
is a unital algebra and
\alpha:A\to A\odot H:a\mapsto a_{(0)}\otimes a_{(1)}
is a unital algebra homomorphism satisfying
(\id\odot\Delta)\alpha=(\alpha\odot\id)\alpha
and
(\id\odot\epsilon)\alpha=\id
. Given a left
A
-module
V
and a left
H
-module
W
, the tensor product
V\odot W
becomes a left
A
-module via

a\rhd(v\otimes w):=(a_{(0)}\rhd v)\otimes(a_{(1)}\rhd w),\quad a\in A,\quad v\in V,\quad w\in W.

The construction of the
W^{*}
-module category
\Rep(A)\curvearrowleft\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
is an analytic version of this well-known algebraic construction.

Remark 4.3
.

If
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(A)
and
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
, there exists a Hilbert space
\mathcal{G}
together with an isometry
\kappa\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{G}\otimes L^{2}(A))
, where
A\curvearrowright\mathcal{G}\otimes L^{2}(A)
via
a\mapsto 1\otimes\pi_{A}(a)
. We then have

\pi_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}}(a)=(\kappa^{*}\otimes 1)\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\alpha,23}^{\mathcal{K}}(1\otimes\pi_{A}(a)\otimes 1)\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\alpha,23}^{\mathcal{K},*}(\kappa\otimes 1),\quad a\in A.

(4.6)

In fact, the formula (
4.6
) can be used to
define
the action
\Rep(A)\curvearrowleft\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
(using this as a starting point, it takes some work to show that it does not depend on the choice of Hilbert space
\mathcal{G}
and the choice of isometry
\kappa
). The advantage of this approach is that it avoids the use of the bidual von Neumann algebra
C^{*}(\check{\mathbb{G}})^{**}
and the ‘coaction’
\alpha^{u}
, but the downside is that the analogy with the algebraic picture (Remark
4.2
) becomes less transparent, and the proofs become harder to read.

Example 4.4
.

(1)

\pi_{L^{2}(A)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}(a)=U_{\alpha}(\pi_{A}(a)\otimes 1)U_{\alpha}^{*}=(\pi_{A}\otimes\id)\alpha(a)
for all
a\in A
.

(2)

If
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(A)
and if
(\mathcal{K},\mathbb{I})
is the trivial
\check{\mathbb{G}}
-representation, then

\pi_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}}(a)=\pi_{\mathcal{H}}(a)\otimes 1
for all
a\in A.

(3)

If
A\curvearrowleft\mathbb{G}
trivially,
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(A)
and
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
, then

\pi_{\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}}(a)=\pi_{\mathcal{H}}(a)\otimes 1
for all
a\in A
.

4.3.
From equivariant correspondence to module functor.

Proposition 4.5
.

For every triple
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K})\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\times\Rep(B)\times\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
, there is a canonical unitary isomorphism

S_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}),(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K}).

These unitaries are natural in
\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}
. Fixing
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
, we have that

\check{P}(\mathcal{G}):=(F_{\mathcal{G}},\{S_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\}_{\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B),\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})})\in\Fun_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(\Rep(B),\Rep(A)).

Given
y\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}{G}_{B}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
, we have

\{\check{P}(y)_{\mathcal{H}}:=y\boxtimes_{B}\id_{\mathcal{H}}:\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H}:x\otimes_{B}\xi\mapsto yx\otimes_{B}\xi\}_{\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B)}\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(F_{\mathcal{G}},F_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}).

In this way, we obtain the functor

\check{P}:\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\to\Fun_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(\Rep(B),\Rep(A)).

Proof.

Fix
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
and
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
. Consider the unitary isomorphism

I_{\mathcal{G}}:\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}L^{2}(B)\to\mathcal{G}:x\otimes_{B}\xi\mapsto x\xi

of
\mathbb{G}
-
A
-
B
-correspondences. Next, we note that there is an
A
-linear unitary

D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}}:\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(L^{2}(B)\lhd\mathcal{K})\cong\mathcal{G}\lhd\mathcal{K}:x\otimes_{B}(\xi\otimes\eta)\mapsto\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathcal{K}}(x\otimes 1)\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta}^{\mathcal{K},*}(\xi\otimes\eta).

It follows from (
4.4
) that

D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}}\circ(1\boxtimes_{B}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1))=(\rho_{\mathcal{G}}(b)\otimes 1)\circ D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}},\quad b\in B.

We then define the
A
-linear unitary

S_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}}:=(I_{\mathcal{G}}^{*}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{K}})\circ D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}}:\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(L^{2}(B)\lhd\mathcal{K})\cong(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}L^{2}(B))\lhd\mathcal{K}.

For all
b\in B
, we have
S_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}}(1\boxtimes_{B}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1))=((1\boxtimes_{B}\rho_{B}(b))\otimes 1)S_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}}
. By the bijectivity of (
2.1
), there exists a unique unitary natural transformation

S_{\mathcal{G},-,\mathcal{K}}:\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(-\lhd\mathcal{K})\implies(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}-)\lhd\mathcal{K}

such that
S_{\mathcal{G},L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}=S_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}}
. We now show that the unitary isomorphisms

S_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}:\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\to(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K},\quad\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B),\quad\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B),\quad\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})

are also natural in the variables
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{K}
. That is, fixing
\mathcal{G},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
,
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B),\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\in\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
,
x\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}_{B}{G}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
and
y\in\mathscr{L}^{\check{\mathbb{G}}}(\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime})
, we need to show that the rectangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\boxtimes_{B}(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x\boxtimes_{B}(1\lhd y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(x\boxtimes_{B}1)\lhd y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}}

(4.7)

commutes. But a direct computation shows that the rectangle (
4.7
) commutes for the generator
\mathcal{H}=L^{2}(B)\in\Rep(B)
. Since both compositions in the rectangle (
4.7
) are natural in the variable
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B)
, it follows from the injectivity of (
2.1
) that (
4.7
) commutes.

Fixing
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
,
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(B)
and
\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\in\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
, we show next that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(\mathcal{H}\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H})\lhd(\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}\mathcal{H})\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}((\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K}))\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1}

(4.8)

commutes. Again, it suffices to show the commutativity for
\mathcal{H}=L^{2}(B)
. To do this, we start by noting that

\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta}^{\mathcal{K}}\in{}_{B}\mathscr{L}(L^{2}(B)\otimes\mathcal{K},L^{2}(B)\lhd\mathcal{K}),

where
B\curvearrowright L^{2}(B)\otimes\mathcal{K}
via
b\mapsto\pi_{B}(b)\otimes 1
. Consider then the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}((L^{2}(B)\lhd\mathcal{K})\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(L^{2}(B)\lhd\mathcal{K}))\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}((L^{2}(B)\otimes\mathcal{K})\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(L^{2}(B)\otimes\mathcal{K}))\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(L^{2}(B)\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}))\otimes\mathcal{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}L^{2}(B))\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{G},L^{2}(B)\lhd\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{G},L^{2}(B)\otimes\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\boxtimes_{B}(\text{\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta}^{\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(1\boxtimes_{B}\text{\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta}^{\mathcal{K}})\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}^{\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(-\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathcal{G},L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{K}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}^{(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}-)\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}}

(4.9)

where the isomorphisms in the lower rectangle are the canonical isomorphisms from (
2.2
). Here, the upper rectangle commutes by naturality in the middle variable, and the lower rectangle commutes by the commutativity of the diagram (
2.3
).
It is straightforward to verify that

\displaystyle C_{L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}^{\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(-\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime})}((x\otimes_{B}(\xi\otimes\eta^{\prime}))\otimes\eta)=x\otimes_{B}(\xi\otimes\eta\otimes\eta^{\prime}),

\displaystyle C_{L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}^{(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}-)\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}((x\otimes_{B}\xi)\otimes\eta^{\prime}\otimes\eta)=(x\otimes_{B}(\xi\otimes\eta))\otimes\eta^{\prime}.

for every
x\in\mathscr{L}_{B}(L^{2}(B),\mathcal{G}),\xi\in L^{2}(B),\eta\in\mathcal{K}
and
\eta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
. It follows from the commutativity of the diagram (
4.9
) that the commutativity of the diagram (
4.8
) for
\mathcal{H}=L^{2}(B)
is equivalent with

(S_{\mathcal{G},L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1)((1\underset{B}{\boxtimes}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta}^{\mathcal{K}})\otimes 1)C_{L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}^{(\mathcal{G}\underset{B}{\boxtimes}-)\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}(S_{\mathcal{G},L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{K}})C_{L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}^{\mathcal{G}\underset{B}{\boxtimes}(-\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}),*}(1\underset{B}{\boxtimes}(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta}^{\mathcal{K},*}\otimes 1))=S_{\mathcal{G},L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}.

(4.10)

Let
x\in\mathscr{L}_{B}(L^{2}(B),\mathcal{G}),y\in B(\mathcal{K}^{\prime}),\xi\in L^{2}(B),\eta\in\mathcal{K}
and
\eta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
. Then

\displaystyle(I_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes 1\otimes 1)C_{L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}^{(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}-)\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime},*}((1\boxtimes_{B}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta}^{\mathcal{K},*})\otimes 1)(D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}}^{*}\otimes 1)(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\mathcal{G},12}^{\mathcal{K}}(x\otimes 1\otimes y)\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta,12}^{\mathcal{K},*}(\xi\otimes\eta\otimes\eta^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=(x\otimes y\otimes 1)\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta,13}^{\mathcal{K},*}(\xi\otimes\eta^{\prime}\otimes\eta).

Consequently,

\displaystyle(I_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes 1\otimes 1)C_{L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}^{(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}-)\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime},*}((1\boxtimes_{B}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta}^{\mathcal{K},*})\otimes 1)(D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}}^{*}\otimes 1)D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}(x\otimes_{B}(\xi\otimes\eta\otimes\eta^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=(I_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes 1\otimes 1)C_{L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}^{(\mathcal{G}\underset{B}{\boxtimes}-)\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime},*}((1\underset{B}{\boxtimes}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta}^{\mathcal{K},*})\otimes 1)(D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}}^{*}\otimes 1)(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\mathcal{G},12}^{\mathcal{K}}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\mathcal{G},13}^{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}(x\otimes 1\otimes 1)\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta,13}^{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},*}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta,12}^{\mathcal{K},*}(\xi\otimes\eta\otimes\eta^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\mathcal{G},12}^{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}(x\otimes 1\otimes 1)\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta,12}^{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},*}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta,13}^{\mathcal{K},*}(\xi\otimes\eta^{\prime}\otimes\eta)

\displaystyle=(D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}\otimes 1)C_{L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}^{\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(-\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}),*}(1\boxtimes_{B}(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta}^{\mathcal{K},*}\otimes 1))(x\otimes_{B}(\xi\otimes\eta\otimes\eta^{\prime})).

This shows that

(I_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes 1\otimes 1)C_{L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}^{(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}-)\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime},*}((1\boxtimes_{B}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta}^{\mathcal{K},*})\otimes 1)(D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}}^{*}\otimes 1)D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}=(D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}\otimes 1)C_{L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}^{\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(-\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}),*}(1\boxtimes_{B}(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta}^{\mathcal{K},*}\otimes 1)).

Taking adjoints and rearranging, we find

(D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1)((1\boxtimes_{B}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta}^{\mathcal{K}})\otimes 1)C_{L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}^{(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}-)\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}(I_{\mathcal{G}}^{*}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}\otimes 1)C_{L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}^{\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}(-\lhd\mathcal{K}^{\prime}),*}(1\boxtimes_{B}(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!_{\beta}^{\mathcal{K},*}\otimes 1))=D_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}.

Multiplying both sides on the left with
I_{\mathcal{G}}^{*}\otimes 1\otimes 1
and plugging in the definition of the maps
S_{\mathcal{G},L^{2}(B),\mathcal{K}}
, we find the equality (
4.10
).
∎

4.4.
From module functor to equivariant correspondence.

Recall that
V_{21}\in B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})
is a unitary
\check{\mathbb{G}}
-representation.

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
F,G\in\Fun_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(\Rep(B),\Rep(A))
.

(1)

Consider
F((L^{2}(B),\pi_{B}))=(\mathcal{G}_{F},\pi_{F})\in\Rep(A)
.
Writing

\rho_{F}(b):=F(\rho_{B}(b))
for
b\in B
and defining

U_{F}:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}\circ F(U_{\beta})\circ S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}\in B(\mathcal{G}_{F}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})),

(4.11)

we have that
(\mathcal{G}_{F},\pi_{F},\rho_{F},U_{F})\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
.

(2)

If
\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(F,G)
, then
\eta_{L^{2}(B)}\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}_{B}{G}(\mathcal{G}_{F},\mathcal{G}_{G}).

(3)

The assignments
F\mapsto(\mathcal{G}_{F},\pi_{F},\rho_{F},U_{F})
and
\eta\mapsto\eta_{L^{2}(B)}

define a functor

\check{Q}:\Fun_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(\Rep(B),\Rep(A))\to\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B).

(4)

\check{Q}\circ\check{P}\cong\id_{\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)}.

Proof.

(1) To simplify notation, we use the following notations in this proof:

\displaystyle S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,1}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})},

\displaystyle S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})},

\displaystyle S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,2}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})},

\displaystyle S_{V_{31}}^{1,2}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})},

\displaystyle S_{V_{32}}^{1,2}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})},

\displaystyle S_{V_{31}V_{32}}^{1,2}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})},

\displaystyle S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21}),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})},

\displaystyle S_{V_{21},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21}),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})},

\displaystyle S_{\mathbb{I,}\mathbb{I}}^{2,1}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I}),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}.

We start by noting that

U_{\beta}\in{}_{B}\mathscr{L}(L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I}),L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})),

since for
b\in B
, we have

U_{\beta}\pi_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}(b)=U_{\beta}(\pi_{B}(b)\otimes 1)=(\pi_{B}\otimes\id)(\beta(b))U_{\beta}=\pi_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}(b)U_{\beta}.

Consequently, it makes sense to define the unitary
U_{F}:\mathcal{G}_{F}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\to\mathcal{G}_{F}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
given by
U_{F}:=S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}\circ F(U_{\beta})\circ S{I}^{1,1,*}
. If
x\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime}=\mathscr{L}^{\check{\mathbb{G}}}((L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21}))
, we have

\displaystyle U_{F}(1\otimes x)

\displaystyle=S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}F(U_{\beta})S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,1,*}(1\otimes x)=S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}F(U_{\beta})F(1\otimes x)S{I}^{1,1,*}=S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}F(1\otimes x)F(U_{\beta})S{I}^{1,1,*}

\displaystyle=(1\otimes x)S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}F(U_{\beta})S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,1,*}=(1\otimes x)U_{F},

and we conclude that
U_{F}\in B(\mathcal{G}_{F})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
. Using the fact that

V\in\mathscr{L}^{\check{\mathbb{G}}}((L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I}),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})),

we then calculate

\displaystyle V_{23}U_{F,12}

\displaystyle=V_{23}(S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}\otimes 1)(F(U_{\beta})\otimes 1)(S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=V_{23}S_{V_{31}}^{1,2}S_{V_{21},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1,*}(F(U_{\beta})\otimes 1)(S{I}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=S_{V_{31}V_{32}}^{1,2}F(V_{23})S_{V_{21},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1,*}(F(U_{\beta})\otimes 1)(S{I}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=S_{V_{31}V_{32}}^{1,2}F(V_{23})F(U_{\beta}\otimes 1)S_{\mathbb{I},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1,*}(S{I}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=S_{V_{31}V_{32}}^{1,2}F(U_{\beta,12}U_{\beta,13}V_{23})S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,2,*}

\displaystyle=(S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}\otimes 1)S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1}F(U_{\beta,12})F(U_{\beta,13})F(V_{23})S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,2,*}

\displaystyle=(S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}\otimes 1)(F(U_{\beta})\otimes 1)S_{\mathbb{I},V_{21}}^{2,1}F(U_{\beta,13})F(V_{23})S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,2,*}

\displaystyle=(S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}\otimes 1)(F(U_{\beta})\otimes 1)(S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)S_{V_{32}}^{1,2}F(U_{\beta,13})F(V_{23})S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,2,*}

\displaystyle=U_{F,12}S_{V_{32}}^{1,2}F(U_{\beta,13})S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,2,*}V_{23}

\displaystyle=U_{F,12}U_{F,13}V_{23},

and it follows that
U_{F}
is a unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation.

By construction, we have that
U_{F}\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G}_{F}\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I}),\mathcal{G}_{F}\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21}))
. Therefore, given
a\in A
, it follows that

\displaystyle U_{F}(\pi_{F}(a)\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=U_{F}\pi_{\mathcal{G}_{F}\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}(a)=\pi_{\mathcal{G}_{F}\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}(a)U_{F}=(\pi_{F}\otimes\id)(\alpha(a))U_{F}.

On the other hand, if
b\in B
, then we have

{}_{B}\mathscr{L}(L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I}))=\rho_{B}(B)\bar{\otimes}B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})),

and thus for
b\in B
and
x\in B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
, we find

S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}F(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x)S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}=F(\rho_{B}(b))\otimes x=\rho_{F}(b)\otimes x,

so by normality of
F
, it follows that

S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}F((\rho_{B}\otimes R)(\beta(b)))S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}=(\rho_{F}\otimes R)(\beta(b)).

Consequently, for
b\in B
,

\displaystyle(\rho_{F}(b)\otimes 1)U_{F}

\displaystyle=(F(\rho_{B}(b))\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}F(U_{\beta})S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}

\displaystyle=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}F((\rho_{B}(b)\otimes 1)U_{\beta})S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}

\displaystyle=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}F(U_{\beta}(\rho_{B}\otimes R)(\beta(b)))S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}

\displaystyle=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}F(U_{\beta})F((\rho_{B}\otimes R)(\beta(b)))S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}

\displaystyle=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}F(U_{\beta})S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}(\rho_{F}\otimes R)(\beta(b))=U_{F}(\rho_{F}\otimes R)(\beta(b)).

We conclude that
(\mathcal{G}_{F},\pi_{F},\rho_{F},U_{F})\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
, as desired.

(2) We calculate

\displaystyle(\eta_{L^{2}(B)}\otimes 1)U_{F}

\displaystyle=(\eta_{L^{2}(B)}\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}F(U_{\beta})S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}

\displaystyle=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}\eta_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}F(U_{\beta})S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}

\displaystyle=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}G(U_{\beta})\eta_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}=U_{G}(\eta_{L^{2}(B)}\otimes 1).

(3) Clear from the two previous steps.

(4) Given
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)
, it suffices to show that
\check{Q}(F_{\mathcal{G}})=(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}L^{2}(B),\pi_{\boxtimes},\rho_{\boxtimes},U_{\boxtimes})
. Consider the unitary identification

T:(\mathcal{G}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\boxtimes_{B\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}(L^{2}(B)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\to(\mathcal{G}\boxtimes_{B}L^{2}(B))\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})

from (
2.6
). With
\mathcal{K}=(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\in\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
and a unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation
U\in B(\mathcal{H})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
, we have
\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!^{\mathcal{K}}=1\otimes 1
and with
\mathcal{K}=(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})\in\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
, we have
\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{U}$}}\>\!^{\mathcal{K}}=U
.
Then we calculate for
x\in\mathscr{L}_{B}(L^{2}(B),\mathcal{G}),\xi\in L^{2}(B)
and
\eta\in L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
that

\displaystyle(I_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes 1)U_{\boxtimes}S_{\mathcal{G},L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}(x\otimes_{B}(\xi\otimes\eta))

\displaystyle=(I_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes 1)U_{\boxtimes}((x\otimes_{B}\xi)\otimes\eta)

\displaystyle=(I_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes 1)T(U_{\mathcal{G}}(x\otimes 1)U_{\beta}^{*}\otimes_{B\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})}U_{\beta}(\xi\otimes\eta))

\displaystyle=U_{\mathcal{G}}(x\otimes 1)U_{\beta}^{*}U_{\beta}(\xi\otimes\eta)

\displaystyle=(I_{\mathcal{G}}\otimes 1)S_{\mathcal{G},L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}(1\boxtimes_{B}U_{\beta})(x\otimes_{B}(\xi\otimes\eta)).

It follows that

U_{\boxtimes}=S_{\mathcal{G},L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}\circ F_{\mathcal{G}}(U_{\beta})\circ S_{\mathcal{G},L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}=U_{F_{\mathcal{G}}}.

∎

4.5.
Equivariant Eilenberg-Watts theorem

Theorem 4.7
.

The restriction functor

\Fun_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(\Rep(B),\Rep(A))\to\Fun_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}}))}(\Rep(B),\Rep(A))

is an isomorphism of categories.

Proof.

This is a straightforward modification of the proof of Theorem
3.5
.
∎

Theorem 4.8

(Equivariant Eilenberg-Watts theorem II)
.

The canonical functors

\displaystyle\check{P}:\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)\to\Fun_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(\Rep(B),\Rep(A)),\quad\check{Q}:\Fun_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(\Rep(B),\Rep(A))\to\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)

are quasi-inverse to each other.

Proof.

Since
\check{Q}\circ\check{P}\cong\id_{\mathrm{Corr}{G}(A,B)}
, it suffices to prove that
\check{Q}
is fully faithful. In other words, given
F,G\in\Fun_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(\Rep(B),\Rep(A))
, we need to prove that the map

\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(F,G)\to{}_{A}\mathscr{L}_{B}{G}(\mathcal{G}_{F},\mathcal{G}_{G}):\eta\mapsto\eta_{L^{2}(B)}

(4.12)

is bijective. We will follow the strategy from Proposition
3.4
for this. Since
L^{2}(B)
is a generator for
\Rep(B)
, it is clear that (
4.12
) is injective. Next, we let
\eta_{1}\in{}_{A}\mathscr{L}{G}_{B}(\mathcal{G}_{F},\mathcal{G}_{G})
and we define the
A
-linear operators

\displaystyle\eta_{2}:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}^{*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})},

\displaystyle\eta_{3}:=S_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21}),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}^{*}(\eta_{2}\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21}),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}.

Note that

\displaystyle{}_{B}\mathscr{L}(L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21}))

\displaystyle=U_{\beta}(\rho_{B}(B)\bar{\otimes}B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})))U_{\beta}^{*},

\displaystyle{}_{B}\mathscr{L}(L^{2}(B)\lhd((L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})))

\displaystyle=U_{\beta,12}U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(B)\bar{\otimes}B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G})))U_{\beta,13}^{*}U_{\beta,12}^{*}.

We then calculate for
b\in B
and
y\in B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
that

\displaystyle\eta_{2}F(U_{\beta}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y)U_{\beta}^{*})

\displaystyle=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}^{*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}F(U_{\beta})F(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y)F(U_{\beta}^{*})

\displaystyle=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}^{*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1)U_{F}S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}F(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y)F(U_{\beta}^{*})

\displaystyle=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}^{*}U_{G}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}F(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y)F(U_{\beta}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta})S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1)(\rho_{F}(b)\otimes y)S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}F(U_{\beta}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta})S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}(\rho_{G}(b)\otimes y)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}F(U_{\beta}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta})G(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y)S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}F(U_{\beta}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta})G(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y)S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1)U_{F}^{*}S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta})G(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y)S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}^{*}U_{G}^{*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1)S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y)U_{\beta}^{*})\eta_{2}.

Next, we claim that for
b\in B
and
x,y\in B(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
, we have

\eta_{3}F(U_{\beta,12}U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*}U_{\beta,12}^{*})=G(U_{\beta,12}U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*}U_{\beta,12}^{*})\eta_{3}.

(4.13)

Indeed, introducing the notations

\displaystyle S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,1}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})},

\displaystyle S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})},

\displaystyle S_{V_{31}V_{32}}^{1,2}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})},

\displaystyle S_{V_{31}}^{1,2}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})},

\displaystyle S_{V_{32}}^{1,2}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})},

\displaystyle S_{\mathbb{I},V_{21}}^{2,1}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I}),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})},

\displaystyle S_{V_{21},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21}),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})},

\displaystyle S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1}

\displaystyle:=S_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21}),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})},

we calculate:

\displaystyle\eta_{3}F(U_{\beta,12}U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*}U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1,*}(\eta_{2}\otimes 1)S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1}F(U_{\beta,12})F(U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1,*}(\eta_{2}\otimes 1)(F(U_{\beta})\otimes 1)S_{\mathbb{I},V_{21}}^{2,1}F(U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1,*}(\eta_{2}\otimes 1)(F(U_{\beta})\otimes 1)(S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)S^{1,2}_{V_{32}}F(U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1,*}(\eta_{2}\otimes 1)(F(U_{\beta})\otimes 1)(S{I}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)S_{V_{32}}^{1,2}F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y\otimes x)U_{\beta,12}^{*})F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1,*}(\eta_{2}\otimes 1)(F(U_{\beta})\otimes 1)(S{I}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)\Sigma_{23}S_{V_{31}}^{1,2}F(U_{\beta,12}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y\otimes x)U_{\beta,12}^{*})F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1,*}(S_{V_{21}}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(U_{F}\otimes 1)\Sigma_{23}S_{V_{31}}^{1,2}F(U_{\beta,12}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y\otimes x)U_{\beta,12}^{*})F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=S_{V_{31}V_{32}}^{1,2,*}(U_{G}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)\Sigma_{23}S_{V_{31}}^{1,2}F(U_{\beta,12}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y\otimes x)U_{\beta,12}^{*})F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=S_{V_{31}V_{32}}^{1,2,*}(S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}\otimes 1)(G(U_{\beta})\otimes 1)(S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)\Sigma_{23}S_{V_{31}}^{1,2}F(U_{\beta,12}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y\otimes x)U_{\beta,12}^{*})F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1,*}(G(U_{\beta})\otimes 1)(S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)\Sigma_{23}S_{V_{31}}^{1,2}F(U_{\beta,12}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y\otimes x)U_{\beta,12}^{*})F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})S_{\mathbb{I},V_{21}}^{2,1,*}(S_{\mathbb{I}}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)\Sigma_{23}S_{V_{31}}^{1,2}F(U_{\beta,12}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y\otimes x)U_{\beta,12}^{*})F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})S_{V_{32}}^{1,2,*}\Sigma_{23}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)S_{V_{31}}^{1,2}F(U_{\beta,12}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y\otimes x)U_{\beta,12}^{*})F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})G(\Sigma_{23})S_{V_{31}}^{1,2,*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)S_{V_{31}}^{1,2}F(U_{\beta,12}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y\otimes x)U_{\beta,12}^{*})F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})G(\Sigma_{23})S_{V_{21},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1,*}(\eta_{2}\otimes 1)S_{V_{21},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1}F(U_{\beta,12}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y\otimes x)U_{\beta,12}^{*})F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})G(\Sigma_{23})S_{V_{21},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1,*}(\eta_{2}\otimes 1)(F(U_{\beta}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y)U_{\beta}^{*})\otimes x)S_{V_{21},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1}F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})G(\Sigma_{23})S_{V_{21},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1,*}(G(U_{\beta}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y)U_{\beta}^{*})\otimes x)(\eta_{2}\otimes 1)S_{V_{21},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1}F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})G(\Sigma_{23})G(U_{\beta,12}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes y\otimes x)U_{\beta,12}^{*})S_{V_{21},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1,*}(\eta_{2}\otimes 1)S_{V_{21},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1}F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})G(U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*})G(\Sigma_{23})S_{V_{21},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1,*}(\eta_{2}\otimes 1)S_{V_{21},\mathbb{I}}^{2,1}F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})G(U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*})G(\Sigma_{23})S_{V_{31}}^{1,2,*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)S_{V_{31}}^{1,2}F(\Sigma_{23})F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})G(U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*})S_{V_{32}}^{1,2,*}(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)S_{V_{32}}^{1,2}F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})G(U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*})S_{\mathbb{I},V_{21}}^{2,1,*}(S{I}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(S{I}^{1,1}\otimes 1)S_{\mathbb{I},V_{21}}^{2,1}F(U_{\beta,12}^{*})

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})G(U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*})S_{\mathbb{I},V_{21}}^{2,1,*}(S{I}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(S{I}^{1,1}\otimes 1)(F(U_{\beta}^{*})\otimes 1)S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1}

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})G(U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*})S_{\mathbb{I},V_{21}}^{2,1,*}(S{I}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(U_{F}^{*}\otimes 1)(S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}\otimes 1)S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1}

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})G(U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*})S_{\mathbb{I},V_{21}}^{2,1,*}(S{I}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)(U_{G}^{*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}\otimes 1)S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1}

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})G(U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*})S_{\mathbb{I},V_{21}}^{2,1,*}(G(U_{\beta}^{*})\otimes 1)(S_{V_{21}}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}\otimes 1)S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1}

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12})G(U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*})G(U_{\beta,12}^{*})S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1,*}(S_{V_{21}}^{1,1,*}\otimes 1)(\eta_{1}\otimes 1\otimes 1)(S_{V_{21}}^{1,1}\otimes 1)S_{V_{21},V_{21}}^{2,1}

\displaystyle=G(U_{\beta,12}U_{\beta,13}(\rho_{B}(b)\otimes x\otimes y)U_{\beta,13}^{*}U_{\beta,12}^{*})\eta_{3}.

It follows from the bijectivity of (
2.1
) that there exists a unique natural transformation
\eta:F\implies G
such that
\eta_{L^{2}(B)\lhd((L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21}))}=\eta_{3}
. Making use of an isometric intertwiner
T:(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})\to(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})
, it follows that
\eta_{L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})}=\eta_{2}
. Since
L^{2}(B)\lhd(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})
generates
\Rep(B)
and since the
\check{\mathbb{G}}
-representation
(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),V_{21})
generates
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}}))
, we find

\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}}))}(F,G)
. We then automatically have
\eta_{L^{2}(B)}=\eta_{1}
as well. By Theorem
4.7
, it follows that

\eta\in\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(L^{\infty}(\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}}))}(F,G)=\operatorname{Nat}_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(F,G)
. Thus, (
4.12
) is surjective, finishing the proof.
∎

It is worth mentioning the following special case of the equivariant Eilenberg-Watts theorem (take
A=B=\mathbb{C}
, which we view as
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebras):

Corollary 4.9
.

Consider the action
\operatorname{Hilb}\curvearrowleft\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
through the forgetful functor. Then we have a canonical equivalence

\Rep(\mathbb{G})\simeq\End_{\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})}(\Hilb)

of
W^{*}
-categories.

The following result is also clear now (compare with Proposition
3.10
):

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
(A,\alpha)
and
(B,\beta)
two
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebras. The following are equivalent:

(1)

(A,\alpha)\sim{G}(B,\beta)
.

(2)

\Rep(A)
and
\Rep(B)
are equivalent as
\Rep(\check{\mathbb{G}})
-
W^{*}
-module categories.

4.6.
Connection between module categories.

The proofs of both versions of the equivariant Eilenberg-Watts theorem are very similar in spirit. This is no coincidence, as there is a strong connection between the relevant module categories, as we will now explain. Consider a
\mathbb{G}
-
W^{*}
-algebra
(A,\alpha)
. In Section
3
, we encountered the natural action
\lhd:\Rep(A^{\rtimes})\curvearrowleft\Rep(\mathbb{G})
. On the other hand, since
(A^{\rtimes},\alpha^{\rtimes})
is a
\check{\mathbb{G}}
-
W^{*}
-algebra, we also have the action
\Rep(A^{\rtimes})\curvearrowleft\Rep(\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}})
. The isomorphism
\mathbb{G}\cong\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}}
given by the map
x\mapsto u{G}xu{G}
induces the isomorphism

\Rep(\mathbb{G})\cong\Rep(\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}}):(\mathcal{K},U)\mapsto(\mathcal{K},(1\otimes u{G})U(1\otimes u{G}))

of
W^{*}
-tensor categories (it acts identically on morphisms), so we obtain an action
\blacktriangleleft\ :\Rep(A^{\rtimes})\curvearrowleft\Rep(\mathbb{G})
in a natural way. We now prove that these module actions coincide.

It will be convenient to introduce the natural map
\eta:C^{*}(\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}})\to C_{0}^{r}(\check{\mathbb{G}})
given by

\eta(\check{\check{\omega}})=(\id\otimes\check{\check{\omega}})(\check{V}_{21})=(\check{\check{\omega}}\otimes\id)(\check{V}),\quad\check{\check{\omega}}\in L_{\sharp}^{1}(\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}}).

We then have

(\id\otimes\eta)(\check{\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!})=\check{V}
. Consequently, the
\check{\mathbb{G}}
-representation
\check{V}_{23}\in M(\mathcal{K}(L^{2}(A)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\check{\mathbb{G}}))
has
\check{\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!}_{23}\in M(\mathcal{K}(L^{2}(A)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\otimes C^{*}(\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}}))
as its lift to the universal level. Consider the universal lift

\alpha^{\rtimes,u}:A^{\rtimes}\to A^{\rtimes}\bar{\otimes}C^{*}(\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}})^{**}

of the coaction
\alpha^{\rtimes}:A^{\rtimes}\to A^{\rtimes}\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})
.
Since
(L^{2}(A)\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\pi_{A}\otimes\id,\check{V}_{23})\in\Rep^{\check{\mathbb{G}}}(A^{\rtimes})
, it follows from (
4.3
) that

\displaystyle(\pi_{A}\otimes\id\otimes\id)\alpha^{\rtimes,u}(z)=\check{\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!}_{23}((\pi_{A}\otimes\id)(z)\otimes 1)\check{\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!}_{23}^{*},\quad z\in A^{\rtimes}.

It then follows from the injectivity of
\pi_{A}\otimes\id\otimes\id
that

\alpha^{\rtimes,u}(\alpha(a))=\alpha(a)\otimes 1,\quad\alpha^{\rtimes,u}(1\otimes\check{y})=1\otimes\check{\Delta}^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}(\check{y}),\quad a\in A,\quad\check{y}\in L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}}),

where
\check{\Delta}^{r,u,\operatorname{VN}}:L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\to L^{\infty}(\check{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}C^{*}(\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}})^{**}
is given by
\check{x}\mapsto\check{\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!}(\check{x}\otimes 1)\check{\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!}^{*}
.

We also have

\displaystyle\check{V}_{12}\check{\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!}_{13}=(\id\otimes\eta\otimes\id)(\id\otimes\Delta_{C^{*}(\check{\check{\mathbb{G}}})})(\check{\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!})=(\id\otimes\check{\Delta}^{r,u})(\id\otimes\eta)(\check{\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!})=(\id\otimes\check{\Delta}^{r,u})(\check{V}).

Consequently, using the identity
\check{V}=(u{G}\otimes 1)V_{21}(u{G}\otimes 1)
, it follows that

(\check{\Delta}^{r,u}\otimes\id)(V)=V_{13}u_{\mathbb{G},3}\check{\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!}_{32}u_{\mathbb{G},3}.

Combining these facts, it follows for
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(A^{\rtimes})
and
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
that

\displaystyle(\pi_{\mathcal{H}\blacktriangleleft\mathcal{K}}\otimes\id)(V_{23})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\tilde{\phi}_{(1\otimes u{G})U_{\mathcal{K}}(1\otimes u{G})}\otimes\id)(\alpha^{\rtimes,u}\otimes\id)(V_{23})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\tilde{\phi}_{(1\otimes u{G})U_{\mathcal{K}}(1\otimes u{G})}\otimes\id)(V_{24}u_{\mathbb{G},4}\check{\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!}_{43}u_{\mathbb{G},4})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\tilde{\phi}_{(1\otimes u{G})U_{\mathcal{K}}(1\otimes u{G})}\otimes\id)(V_{24})(\pi_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\tilde{\phi}_{(1\otimes u{G})U_{\mathcal{K}}(1\otimes u{G})}\otimes\id)(u_{\mathbb{G},4}\check{\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}\>\!}_{43}u_{\mathbb{G},4})

\displaystyle=U_{\pi_{\mathcal{H}},13}U_{\mathcal{K},23}.

Since also
\pi_{\mathcal{H}\blacktriangleleft\mathcal{K}}(\alpha(a))=\pi_{\mathcal{H}}(\alpha(a))\otimes 1
for all
a\in A
, we conclude that

\mathcal{H}\blacktriangleleft\mathcal{K}=\mathcal{H}\lhd\mathcal{K},\quad\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(A^{\rtimes}),\quad\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G}).

5.
Drinfeld center of
\Rep(\mathbb{G})

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a locally compact quantum group. Instead of working with
right

\mathbb{G}
-representations as in the previous sections, it will turn out to be very convenient to switch conventions and to work with
left

\mathbb{G}
-representations instead. To be very concrete, in this section
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
denotes the
W^{*}
-tensor category such that:

•

Objects are pairs
(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}})
where
\mathcal{H}
is a Hilbert space and
U_{\mathcal{H}}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}B(\mathcal{H})
is a unitary such that
(\Delta\otimes\id)(U_{\mathcal{H}})=U_{\mathcal{H},13}U_{\mathcal{H},23}
.

•

If
\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, we put
\mathscr{L}^{\mathbb{G}}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime})=\{x\in B(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{H}^{\prime}):(1\otimes x)U=U^{\prime}(1\otimes x)\}
for the associated morphism space of intertwiners.

•

The tensor product is given by

(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}})\otop(\mathcal{K},U_{\mathcal{K}}):=(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K},U_{\mathcal{H},12}U_{\mathcal{K},13}).

When we write
L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
in this section, we always mean
(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),W)
, unless explicitly mentioned otherwise.

We denote the Drinfeld double of
\mathbb{G}
by
D(\mathbb{G})
and we write
\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
for the Drinfeld center of the
W^{*}
-tensor category
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
. The main goal of this section is to prove that there is a canonical isomorphism
\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))\cong\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
of braided
W^{*}
-tensor categories. This result was already established for compact quantum groups in
[
NY18
]
*Section 3. First, we give some preliminaries in which we recall the relevant definitions.

5.1.
Universal quantum groups

The theory of universal quantum groups was developed in
[
Kus01
]
. Since we work in ‘left’ conventions, we will use the more common notations

C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G}):=C^{*}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})
and
C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}):=C^{*}(\mathbb{G}).
We also consider the universal versions

\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!\in M(C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})),\quad\mathds{W}\in M(C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})),\quad{\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}\in M(C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}))

of the multiplicative unitary
W\in M(C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}))
, as well as the
canonical surjective
*
-homomorphisms

\pi{G}:C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\to C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G}),\quad\pi_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}}:C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\to C_{0}^{r}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}).

The universal versions

\Delta^{u}:C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\to M(C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})),\quad\hat{\Delta}^{u}:C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\to M(C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}))

of the coproducts satisfy
(\Delta^{u}\otimes\id)({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}})={\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}_{13}{\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}_{23}
and
(\id\otimes\hat{\Delta}^{u})({\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}})={\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}_{13}{\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}_{12}
.
The assignments

\displaystyle(\mathcal{H},U)\mapsto[\phi_{U}=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}:C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\to B(\mathcal{H}):L_{\sharp}^{1}(\mathbb{G})\ni\omega\mapsto(\omega\otimes\id)(U)],\quad\phi\mapsto(\id\otimes\phi)(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!)

set up a bijection between the unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representations on Hilbert spaces and the non-degenerate
*
-representations of
C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})
on Hilbert spaces. It is important to note that in these conventions, we have

\phi_{\mathcal{H}\otop\mathcal{K}}:=(\phi_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{K}})\circ\hat{\Delta}^{u,\mathrm{op}},\quad\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G}).

5.2.
Drinfeld double

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a locally compact quantum group. Its
Drinfeld double

D(\mathbb{G})

[
BV05
,
NV10
]
(see also
[
DCK24
]
*Section 2.3) is the locally compact quantum group with
L^{\infty}(D(\mathbb{G})):=L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})
and coproduct given by

\Delta_{D(\mathbb{G})}(z):=W_{32}\Sigma_{23}(\Delta\otimes\hat{\Delta})(z)\Sigma_{23}W_{32}^{*},\quad z\in L^{\infty}(D(\mathbb{G})).

The locally compact quantum groups
\mathbb{G}
and
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
are closed quantum subgroups of
D(\mathbb{G})
(in the sense of Vaes
[
DKSS12
]
) in a canonical way (see e.g.
[
DKV24
]
*Lemma 7.13 for details), meaning there are canonical unital, isometric, normal coproduct-preserving
*
-homomorphisms

\gamma_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}:L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\to L^{\infty}(\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})}),\quad\gamma_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}:L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\to L^{\infty}(\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})}).

They automatically restrict to non-degenerate
*
-homomorphisms (see
[
DKSS12
]
*Theorem 3.3)

\gamma_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}:C_{0}^{r}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\to M(C_{0}^{r}(\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})})),\quad\gamma_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}:C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G})\to M(C_{0}^{r}(\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})})),

and these admit the universal versions

\gamma_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}^{u}:C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\to M(C_{0}^{u}(\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})}));\quad\gamma_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}^{u}:C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\to M(C_{0}^{u}(\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})})),

satisfying

\gamma_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}\circ\pi_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}}=\pi_{\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})}}\circ\gamma_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}^{u}
and
\gamma_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}\circ\pi_{\mathbb{G}}=\pi_{\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})}}\circ\gamma_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}^{u}
.

By a
Yetter-Drinfeld representation
of
\mathbb{G}
, we mean a triple
(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}},\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}})
such that

\mathcal{H}
is a Hilbert space,

U_{\mathcal{H}}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\bar{\otimes}B(\mathcal{H})
is a unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation,
\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}}\in L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}B(\mathcal{H})
is a unitary
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
-representation
and the Yetter-Drinfeld commutation relation

W_{12}U_{\mathcal{H},13}\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H},23}W_{12}^{*}=\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H},23}U_{\mathcal{H},13}

(5.1)

holds. Note that (
5.1
) is an identity in the multiplier
C^{*}
-algebra
M(C_{0}^{r}(\mathbb{G})\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}))
. The assignment

(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}},\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}})\mapsto U_{\mathcal{H},13}\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H},23}
defines a bijection between the Yetter-Drinfeld representations of
\mathbb{G}
and the
D(\mathbb{G})
-representations. Its inverse is constructed as follows: if
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))
, we obtain the non-degenerate
*
-representation
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}\circ\gamma_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}^{u}:C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\to B(\mathcal{H})
that corresponds to a unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representation on
\mathcal{H}
, and similarly
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}\circ\gamma_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}^{u}:C_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\to B(\mathcal{H})
corresponds to a unitary
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
-representation on
\mathcal{H}
.

The above assignment is compatible with tensor products, in the sense that

(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}},\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}})\otop_{D(\mathbb{G})}(\mathcal{K},U_{\mathcal{K}},\hat{U}_{\mathcal{K}})=(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K},U_{\mathcal{H}}\otop{G}U_{\mathcal{K}},\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}}\otop_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}}\hat{U}_{\mathcal{K}}).

5.3.
Drinfeld center

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a locally compact quantum group. Consider the associated
W^{*}
-tensor category
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
of unitary
\mathbb{G}
-representations on Hilbert spaces. Its
Drinfeld center

\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
is the
W^{*}
-category of
unitary half-braidings
. More precisely, objects in this category consist of pairs
(\mathcal{H},C_{\mathcal{H},-})
where
\mathcal{H}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
and
C_{\mathcal{H},-}:\mathcal{H}\otop-\implies-\otop\mathcal{H}
is a natural unitary isomorphism in
\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, such that the braid relation

C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{L}}=(\id_{\mathcal{K}}\otimes C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{L}})\circ(C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\otimes\id_{\mathcal{L}}),\quad\mathcal{K},\mathcal{L}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})

(5.2)

is satisfied.

A morphism from the object
(\mathcal{H},C_{\mathcal{H},-})
to the object
(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},D_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},-})
consists of an intertwiner
x:\mathcal{H}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}
of
\mathbb{G}
-representations such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}\otimes\mathcal{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}\otimes\mathcal{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},\mathcal{K}}}

commutes for all
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
.

Given
(\mathcal{H},C_{\mathcal{H},-}),(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},D_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},-})\in\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
, we also have

(\mathcal{H}\otop\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},(C_{\mathcal{H},-}\otimes\id_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}})\circ(\id_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes D_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},-}))\in\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G})).

In this way,
\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
becomes a
W^{*}
-tensor category. Moreover, the unitaries

C_{\mathcal{H},\tilde{\mathcal{H}}}:(\mathcal{H},C_{\mathcal{H},-})\otimes(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},D_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},-})\to(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},D_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},-})\otimes(\mathcal{H},C_{\mathcal{H},-})

turn
\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
into a braided
W^{*}
-tensor category.

5.4.
From representation to half-braiding.

Let
(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}},\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}})\in\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))
. Given
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, we consider the associated non-degenerate
*
-homomorphism
\phi_{\mathcal{K}}:C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\to B(\mathcal{K})
, as well as the half-lifted version
\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}}\in M(C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}))
of
\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}}\in M(C_{0}^{r}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}))
. Similarly as in the previous section, we will employ the notation

\hat{\mathds{U}}^{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{H}}:=(\phi_{\mathcal{K}}\otimes\id)(\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}})\in B(\mathcal{K}\otimes\mathcal{H}),

and we define the unitaries

C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}:=\hat{\mathds{U}}^{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{H}}\circ\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}:\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K}\to\mathcal{K}\otimes\mathcal{H},\quad\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G}).

Let us now verify that
(\mathcal{H},C_{\mathcal{H},-})\in\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
. It is easily verified that these unitaries are natural in
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
. Next, we note that
C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}
is an intertwiner of
\mathbb{G}
-representations if and only if

\hat{\mathds{U}}^{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{H},23}U_{\mathcal{H},13}=U_{\mathcal{K},12}U_{\mathcal{H},13}\hat{\mathds{U}}^{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{H},23}U_{\mathcal{K},12}^{*}.

(5.3)

We now prove (
5.3
).
We consider the half-lifted comultiplication

\hat{\Delta}^{r,u}:C_{0}^{r}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\to M(C_{0}^{r}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes C_{0}^{u}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}))
uniquely determined by

\hat{\Delta}^{r,u}\circ\pi_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}}=(\pi_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\id)\circ\hat{\Delta}^{u}.

Applying
\id\otimes\hat{\Delta}^{r,u}\otimes\id
to the identity (
5.1
) and using the identities
(\id\otimes\hat{\Delta}^{r,u})(W)=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{13}W_{12}
and
(\hat{\Delta}^{r,u}\otimes\id)(\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}})=\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H},13}\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H},23}
, we find

\displaystyle\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{13}W_{12}U_{\mathcal{H},14}\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H},24}\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H},34}W_{12}^{*}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{13}^{*}=\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H},24}\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H},34}U_{\mathcal{H},14}.

Rearranging and invoking (
5.1
), we find

\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H},24}U_{\mathcal{H},14}W_{12}\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H},34}W_{12}^{*}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{13}^{*}=W_{12}U_{\mathcal{H},14}\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H},24}\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H},34}W_{12}^{*}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{13}^{*}=\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H},24}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{13}^{*}\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H},34}U_{\mathcal{H},14}.

Making the obvious cancellations and leaving out the second leg leads to the identity

U_{\mathcal{H},13}\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H},23}\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{12}^{*}=\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!_{12}^{*}\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H},23}U_{\mathcal{H},13}.

Rearranging, applying the map
\id\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{K}}\otimes\id
to this equation, and keeping in mind that
(\id\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{K}})(\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{W}$}}\>\!)=U_{\mathcal{K}}
, the equation (
5.3
) readily follows.

Finally, given
\mathcal{K},\mathcal{L}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, we compute

\displaystyle C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=(\phi_{\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{L}}\otimes\id)(\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}})\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otimes\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=(\phi_{\mathcal{K}}\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{L}}\otimes\id)(\hat{\Delta}^{u,\mathrm{op}}\otimes\id)(\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}})\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otimes\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=(\phi_{\mathcal{K}}\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{L}}\otimes\id)(\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H},23}\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H},13})\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otimes\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=(\phi_{\mathcal{L}}\otimes\id)(\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}})_{23}(\phi_{\mathcal{K}}\otimes\id)(\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}})_{13}\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otimes\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=(\phi_{\mathcal{L}}\otimes\id)(\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}})_{23}(1\otimes\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{L}})(1\otimes\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{L}}^{*})(\phi_{\mathcal{K}}\otimes\id)(\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}})_{13}\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otimes\mathcal{L}}=(1\otimes C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{L}})(C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1).

We conclude that
(\mathcal{H},C_{\mathcal{H},-})\in\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
. If
(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}},\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}}),(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},U_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}},\hat{U}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}})\in\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))
, and
x:\mathcal{H}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}
is an intertwiner of
D(\mathbb{G})
-representations, then
x
is both an intertwiner of the corresponding
\mathbb{G}
-representations and
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
-representations. From this, we deduce that
x
is a morphism in
\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
between the corresponding unitary half-braidings. Thus, we obtain a canonical functor

F:\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))\to\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))

that is the identity on morphism spaces.

5.5.
From half-braiding to representation.

We will require the following lemma:

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
(\mathcal{H},C_{\mathcal{H},-})\in\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
. If
\mathcal{K}
is a Hilbert space, endowed with the trivial
\mathbb{G}
-representation, then
C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}=\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}
.

Proof.

If
x\in B(\mathcal{K})
, then by naturality of
C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}
in the second variable, we find

C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\Sigma_{\mathcal{K},\mathcal{H}}(x\otimes 1)=C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}(1\otimes x)\Sigma_{\mathcal{K},\mathcal{H}}=(x\otimes 1)C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\Sigma_{\mathcal{K},\mathcal{H}}.

From this, it follows that
C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}=(1\otimes y)\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}
for some unitary
y\in B(\mathcal{H})
. We now prove that
y=1
. We first do this in case that
\mathcal{K}=\mathbb{C}
is one-dimensional. In that case, writing
C_{\mathcal{H},\mathbb{C}}=(1\otimes y)\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathbb{C}}
, we consider the following diagram, where
\mathcal{L}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
is fixed:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathcal{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{L}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{L}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathcal{H},\mathbb{C}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathcal{H},\mathbb{C}\otop\mathcal{L}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{L}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

Here, the middle triangle commutes by the braid relation (
5.2
) and the left outer subdiagram commutes by naturality in the second variable. The two remaining outer subdiagrams commute trivially. The take-away from this is that the outer part of the diagram commutes, forcing
y=1
.

Back to the general case, we fix an isometry
t:\mathbb{C}\to\mathcal{K}
, which is of course an intertwiner of
\mathbb{G}
-representations. By naturality in the second variable, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}\otimes\mathcal{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathcal{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}=(1\otimes y)\Sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathcal{H},\mathbb{C}}=\Sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t\otimes 1}

which forces
t\otimes y=t\otimes 1
, whence
y=1
.∎

If
(\mathcal{H},C_{\mathcal{H},-})\in\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
, let us verify that
(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}},\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}}:=C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\circ\Sigma_{L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathcal{H}})\in\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))
. Indeed, we first note that if
\hat{x}^{\prime}\in L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})^{\prime}=\mathcal{L}^{\mathbb{G}}(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
, then by naturality in the second variable

\displaystyle\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}}(\hat{x}^{\prime}\otimes 1)

\displaystyle=C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}(1\otimes\hat{x}^{\prime})\Sigma_{L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathcal{H}}=(\hat{x}^{\prime}\otimes 1)C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\Sigma_{L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathcal{H}}=(\hat{x}^{\prime}\otimes 1)\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}},

and it follows that
\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}}\in L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}B(\mathcal{H}).
Next, we verify that
\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}}
is a
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
-representation. To do this, we calculate

\displaystyle\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H},23}\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H},13}W_{12}

\displaystyle=(1\otimes C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})})(1\otimes\Sigma)C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),13}\Sigma_{13}W_{12}

\displaystyle=(1\otimes C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})})(C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes 1)(\Sigma\otimes 1)(1\otimes\Sigma)W_{12}

\displaystyle=C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}(\Sigma\otimes 1)(1\otimes\Sigma)W_{12}

\displaystyle=C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}W_{23}(\Sigma\otimes 1)(1\otimes\Sigma)

\displaystyle=W_{12}C_{\mathcal{H},(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),W)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I})}(\Sigma\otimes 1)(1\otimes\Sigma)

\displaystyle=W_{12}(1\otimes\Sigma)(C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes 1)(\Sigma\otimes 1)(1\otimes\Sigma)=W_{12}\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H},13}.

Here, the third equality follows from the braid equation (
5.2
). The fifth equation follows from the fact that
W\in\mathcal{L}{G}((L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),W)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathbb{I}),(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),W)\otop(L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),W))
and the naturality in the second variable. The sixth equality follows from the braid relation (
5.2
) combined with Lemma
5.1
.

Finally, from the fact that
C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}:\mathcal{H}\otimes L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\otimes\mathcal{H}
is an intertwiner of
\mathbb{G}
-representations, it follows that

\displaystyle W_{12}U_{\mathcal{H},13}\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H},23}W_{12}^{*}

\displaystyle=W_{12}U_{\mathcal{H},13}(1\otimes C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})})(1\otimes\Sigma)W_{12}^{*}

\displaystyle=(1\otimes C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})})U_{\mathcal{H},12}W_{13}(1\otimes\Sigma)W_{12}^{*}

\displaystyle=(1\otimes C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})})U_{\mathcal{H},12}(1\otimes\Sigma)

\displaystyle=(1\otimes C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})})(1\otimes\Sigma)U_{\mathcal{H},13}=\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H},23}U_{\mathcal{H},13},

so that
(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}},\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}})\in\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))
.

If
(\mathcal{H},C_{\mathcal{H},-}),(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},D_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},-})\in\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
and if
x:\mathcal{H}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}
is a morphism between these objects in
\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
(which, by definition, is an intertwiner of
\mathbb{G}
-representations), then we also have

\displaystyle(1\otimes x)C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\Sigma=D_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}(x\otimes 1)\Sigma=D_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\Sigma(1\otimes x),

so that
x
is also an intertwiner of the associated
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
-representations. As such,
x
is an intertwiner of the associated
D(\mathbb{G})
-representations. It is then clear that we obtain a functor

G:\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))\to\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))

which acts identically on morphisms.

5.6.
Isomorphism of W*-tensor categories

Lemma 5.2
.

If
(\mathcal{H},C_{\mathcal{H},-}),(\mathcal{H},D_{\mathcal{H},-})\in\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
and
C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}=D_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}
, then
C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}=D_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}
for all
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
.

Proof.

Since
L^{2}(\mathbb{G})
is a generator for
\Rep(L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}))\subseteq\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, it follows that

C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}=D_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}
for all
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}})).

If
\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(\mathbb{G})
, then
\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})\in\Rep(L^{\infty}(\hat{\mathbb{G}}))
, and thus the braid relation (
5.2
) implies that

(1\otimes C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})})(C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1)=C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}=D_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\otop L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}=(1\otimes D_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})})(D_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1)=(1\otimes C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})})(D_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}\otimes 1),

from which it follows that
C_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}=D_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}.

∎

Theorem 5.3
.

The functors
F
and
G
are inverse to each other, i.e.
F\circ G=\id_{\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))}
and
G\circ F=\id_{\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))}.
Moreover,
F\circ\otop=\otimes\circ(F\times F)
and
G\circ\otimes=\otop\circ(G\times G)
. Thus,
\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))\cong\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))
as
W^{*}
-tensor categories.

Proof.

Starting from
(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}},\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}})\in\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))
, we associate to it

(\mathcal{H},\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}}^{-}\circ\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},-})\in\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
, which in turn gives rise to the
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
-representation

\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}}^{L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\circ\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\circ\Sigma_{L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathcal{H}}=(\phi_{L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\id)(\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}})=(\pi_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\id)(\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}})=\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}}.

Therefore,
G\circ F=\id_{\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))}.

Conversely, given
(\mathcal{H},C_{\mathcal{H},-})\in\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
, the associated
\hat{\mathbb{G}}
-representation

\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}}:=C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\Sigma_{L^{2}(\mathbb{G}),\mathcal{H}}
induces the unitary half-braiding

(\mathcal{H},\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}}^{-}\circ\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},-})
.
We then have

\hat{\mathds{U}}^{L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}_{\mathcal{H}}\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}=(\phi_{L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\id)(\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}})\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}=(\pi_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\id)(\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}})\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}=\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}}\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})}=C_{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathbb{G})},

so Lemma
5.2
implies that
F\circ G=\id_{\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))}
. Verifying the identities
F\circ\otop=\otimes\circ(F\times F)
and
G\circ\otimes=\otop\circ(G\times G)
is routine.
∎

5.7.
Compatibility with the braiding.

As we have seen,
\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
is naturally a braided
W^{*}
-tensor category. On the other hand, the locally compact quantum group
D(\mathbb{G})
is quasi-triangular with respect to the
R
-matrix

\hat{R}:=(\gamma_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\gamma_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})})(\hat{W})\in M(C_{0}^{r}(\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})})\otimes C_{0}^{r}(\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})}))

(see
[
DCK24
]
*Remark 2.4, Proposition 2.7). Considering its universal lift
\hat{R}^{u}\in M(C_{0}^{u}(\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})})\otimes C_{0}^{u}(\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})}))
, uniquely determined by
(\pi_{\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})}}\otimes\pi_{\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})}})(\hat{R}^{u})=\hat{R}

[
MW12
]
*Proposition 4.14, it follows from
[
DCK24
]
*Proposition 3.4 that the unitaries

(\phi_{\mathcal{K}}\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{H}})(\hat{R}^{u})\circ\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}:\mathcal{H}\otop\mathcal{K}\to\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{H},\quad\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))

(5.4)

turn
\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))
into a braided
W^{*}
-tensor category.

Proposition 5.4
.

The monoidal functors
F,G
preserve the braiding. Thus,
\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))\cong\mathcal{Z}(\Rep(\mathbb{G}))
as braided
W^{*}
-tensor categories.

Proof.

The universal lift
\hat{R}^{u}
is explicitly given by

\hat{R}^{u}=(\gamma_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}^{u}\otimes\gamma_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}^{u})(\widehat{{\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}})\in M(C_{0}^{u}(\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})})\otimes C_{0}^{u}(\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})})),

because we have

\displaystyle(\pi_{\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})}}\otimes\pi_{\widehat{D(\mathbb{G})}})(\hat{R}^{u})=(\gamma_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}\otimes\gamma_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})})(\pi_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\pi_{\mathbb{G}})(\widehat{{\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}})=\hat{R}.

Consequently, given
(\mathcal{H},U_{\mathcal{H}},\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}}),(\mathcal{K},U_{\mathcal{K}},\hat{U}_{\mathcal{K}})\in\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))
, we can calculate

\displaystyle(\phi_{\mathcal{K}}\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{H}})(\hat{R}^{u})

\displaystyle=(\phi_{\mathcal{K}}\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{H}})(\gamma_{\mathbb{G}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}^{u}\otimes\gamma_{\hat{\mathbb{G}}\subseteq D(\mathbb{G})}^{u})(\widehat{{\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}})

\displaystyle=(\phi_{U_{\mathcal{K}}}\otimes\phi_{\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}}})(\widehat{{\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}})

\displaystyle=(\phi_{U_{\mathcal{K}}}\otimes\id)(\id\otimes\phi_{\hat{U}_{\mathcal{H}}})(\widehat{{\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}})

\displaystyle=(\phi_{U_{\mathcal{K}}}\otimes\id)(\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}}).

Thus, the braiding (
5.4
) on
\Rep(D(\mathbb{G}))
is given by the unitaries

(\phi_{U_{\mathcal{K}}}\otimes\id)(\hat{\mathds{U}}_{\mathcal{H}})\circ\Sigma_{\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}}:\mathcal{H}\otop\mathcal{K}\to\mathcal{K}\otop\mathcal{H},\quad\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K}\in\Rep(D(\mathbb{G})).

As such, it is clear that
F
and
G
preserve the braiding.
∎
